# SAM's functions and their proofs

> **What this page is:** every function SAM promises, with the tests that
> prove it, the code that carries it, and the scripted checks that try it on
> real apps or in the window. A test may prove several functions. The page
> is generated from [functions.toml](functions.toml) by
> `scripts/function-map.py`; CI checks that every test belongs to a function
> and every function has a test or a stated gap.
>
> Test results are from the newest version report (2026-11-21 05:11:03).

**42 functions:** 3 proven by passing tests, 38 with new tests not yet in a report, 0 with a failing test, 1 by scripted checks, 0 with a gap, 1193 tests in all.

| 42 | [SAM is checked and released as it says: the release key signs only files the release build of that very tag made, source files that match the package recipe and checksum lists the signing script writes itself; a failed release build leaves no tag; the owner's approval counts only for the version the owner saw and is judged by the base branch's checker; the CI scripts fail when the tests could not run; the programs SAM ships contain none of its test and release tools, and every path that builds or uses a program SAM ships or signs (the AppImage build, which the release workflow runs through make appimage, the release and signing scripts and the package recipes' build steps, which builders cannot skip like their test steps, but not the Makefile's cli target, which a developer runs for local use) refuses a built program that follows SAM's developer variables, checked with an empty environment and every folder in a temporary folder; and every workflow keeps pinned actions, least rights and checked downloads.](#42) |  | 26 | ✅ 🆕 26 new tests since the report |

## A. Bringing an AppImage in

<a id="1"></a>
### 1. SAM finds AppImages in the folders you choose and shows them, sandboxed or not, without running anything.

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`peeks_name_and_icon_without_extracting`](../crates/sam-intake/src/peek.rs#L264) | The name and icon of an AppImage are read without unpacking or running it. | ✅ |
| [`the_appimage_behind_a_foreign_menu_entry_is_found`](../crates/sam-intake/src/menu.rs#L360) | The AppImage a foreign menu entry starts is found in its Exec line, also behind env and with quotes. | ✅ |
| [`app_ids_stay_unique_when_names_collide`](../crates/sam-intake/src/id.rs#L98) | App IDs are stable and unique, also when two AppImages have the same name. | ✅ |
| [`found_appimages_get_a_menu_entry_only_with_a_sandbox`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/overview.rs#L13) | AppImages are listed before they have a sandbox; only creating the sandbox adds SAM's menu entry and hides unsandboxed ones, and removing it puts them back. | ✅ |
| [`the_folder_watcher_ignores_hidden_files_and_unfinished_downloads`](../crates/sam-gtk/src/window.rs#L2034) | The folder watcher reacts to AppImages and ignores hidden files and downloads in progress. | ✅ |
| [`a_reader_gone_wrong_comes_to_nothing`](../crates/sam-intake/tests/preview_sandbox.rs#L50) | A reader gone wrong comes to nothing. | ✅ |
| [`the_overview_reads_names_inside_a_sandbox`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/odd_names.rs#L252) | The overview reads an AppImage's name inside the preview sandbox, not in SAM's own process (audit I-F7): a recording stand-in in front of the real Bubblewrap sees the reader start, and the name still arrives. | ✅ |
| [`dropped_pipes_and_icon_bombs_never_reach_gtk`](../crates/sam-gtk/src/window.rs#L1966) | A named pipe dropped on the window is no AppImage, and checking it does not block the window until something writes to it (audit G-F16). | ✅ |
| [`scan_and_discover_say_which_appimages_have_a_sandbox`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/commands.rs#L171) | `scan` and `discover` on the terminal say of each AppImage whether it has a sandbox (and which), is a moved copy of a sandboxed one, or has none: a sandboxed app is never shown as new or unprotected, a new one never as sandboxed. | 🆕 new |
| [`onboarding_is_changed_only_by_done_and_reset`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/commands.rs#L714) | `onboarding` answers whether the first-start tour is done without changing it; `done` and `reset` change it. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_menu_entry_naming_a_pipe_does_not_hang_the_search`](../crates/sam-intake/src/menu.rs#L376) ⟲ | Regression (audit I-F15): a menu entry whose `Exec=` names a named pipe hung the overview, which opened it to look for an AppImage and waited for a writer that never came. | 🆕 new |

**Code:** [`sam-intake/src/peek.rs`](../crates/sam-intake/src/peek.rs), [`sam-intake/src/menu.rs`](../crates/sam-intake/src/menu.rs), [`sam-intake/src/id.rs`](../crates/sam-intake/src/id.rs), [`sam-runner/src/ops/overview.rs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/overview.rs), [`sam-gtk/src/window.rs`](../crates/sam-gtk/src/window.rs)

<a id="2"></a>
### 2. SAM recognizes the AppImage formats in use today (type 2 with a SquashFS or DwarFS image, and the old, rare type 1, which bsdtar unpacks) and refuses files that aren't AppImages, or are for the wrong processor, with a clear message.

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`plain_elf_is_not_appimage`](../crates/sam-detect/src/appimage.rs#L398) | An ordinary program file is not taken for an AppImage. | ✅ |
| [`a_type_2_appimage_is_recognized`](../crates/sam-detect/src/appimage.rs#L406) | A type 2 AppImage (ELF with the AppImage magic and a squashfs image) is recognized. | ✅ |
| [`an_appimage_with_a_dwarfs_image_is_recognized`](../crates/sam-detect/src/appimage.rs#L471) | An AppImage with a DwarFS image instead of squashfs is recognized. | ✅ |
| [`a_file_that_is_no_program_is_not_an_appimage`](../crates/sam-detect/src/appimage.rs#L492) | A file that is not an ELF program is not taken for an AppImage. | ✅ |
| [`appimages_run_only_on_a_matching_processor`](../crates/sam-detect/src/appimage.rs#L507) | An AppImage runs on the same processor and 32-bit builds on their 64-bit relatives; ARM builds never run on x86-64 or the other way. | ✅ |
| [`appimages_with_every_compression_unpack`](../crates/sam-intake/src/testkit.rs#L257) | Every SquashFS compression AppImages use is unpacked by the built-in reader, so none needs the AppImage's own extractor. | ✅ |
| [`an_appimage_for_another_processor_gets_no_sandbox`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/wrong_processor.rs#L14) | An AppImage for ARM is refused on an x86-64 computer with a sentence that names both processors and what to download instead, and nothing is stored (regression: SAM created a sandbox for an arm64 Neovim and failed only at the start, with "permission denied or wrong format"). | ✅ |
| [`an_appimage_shorter_than_its_image_is_incomplete`](../crates/sam-detect/src/appimage.rs#L434) | A download that broke off is recognized: the file is shorter than its SquashFS image says, and a complete one is not. | ✅ |
| [`a_download_that_broke_off_is_refused_and_nothing_is_kept`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/incomplete.rs#L9) | A download that broke off is refused and nothing is kept. | ✅ |
| [`the_host_tools_unpack_what_sams_own_reader_cannot`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/unpack.rs#L335) | The host's unpacking tools unpack what SAM's own reader cannot, inside the unpack sandbox, with the options SAM gives them (contract unpack-tools; audit D-M10: its tests saw only SAM's own reader). | 🆕 new |

**Code:** [`sam-detect/src/appimage.rs`](../crates/sam-detect/src/appimage.rs), [`sam-intake/src/extract.rs`](../crates/sam-intake/src/extract.rs), [`sam-runner/src/ops/add.rs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/add.rs)

**Also tried by:** [`tests/real-apps/run.sh`](../tests/real-apps/run.sh)

<a id="3"></a>
### 3. SAM unpacks an AppImage safely: without running it, inside a sandbox, with limits on size, number of files and time, and with a check of free disk space.

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`an_appimages_own_extractor_cannot_open_the_kernels_rarely_used_doors`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/kernel_surface.rs#L256) | The AppImage's own extractor runs code from the AppImage: here a "runtime" that probes the doors when asked to unpack itself, after SAM's own reader failed on it and the person allowed the fallback. | ✅ |
| [`the_self_unpack_question_names_the_sandbox_and_the_reason`](../crates/sam-intake/src/extract.rs#L1164) | The question before the AppImage unpacks itself says what the sandbox holds back, including the kernel limits (audit I-F17: it named only the network and the files), and why SAM could not. | ✅ |
| [`an_unpacking_tool_cannot_open_the_kernels_rarely_used_doors`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/kernel_surface.rs#L181) | An unpacking tool cannot open the kernels rarely used doors. | ✅ |
| [`unpacking_refuses_to_start_without_enough_free_space`](../crates/sam-intake/src/extract.rs#L1206) | Unpacking does not start when the folder clearly has too little room; the message names the folder, both sizes, and where to choose another folder. | ✅ |
| [`a_full_disk_during_unpacking_is_recognized`](../crates/sam-intake/src/extract.rs#L1237) | A full disk during unpacking is recognized, also inside an error chain, so SAM can clean up and say what happened. | ✅ |
| [`a_failed_swap_keeps_the_old_app`](../crates/sam-intake/src/extract.rs#L1250) ⟲ | Regression (audit I-F16): putting a new unpack in place was two renames, and when the second failed the app had no unpacked files left. | 🆕 new |
| [`an_unpack_box_with_locked_folders_is_removed`](../crates/sam-intake/src/extract.rs#L1282) ⟲ | Regression (review 2026-11-23, F1): a stopped unpack left folders that nobody may enter (modes from the image), which `remove_dir_all` cannot empty, so the partial output stayed on the disk. | 🆕 new |
| [`every_unpacking_tool_meets_the_limits_while_it_runs`](../crates/sam-intake/tests/tool_limits.rs#L49) | Every unpacking tool meets the limits while it runs. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_full_disk_in_the_unpack_sandbox_stops_unpacking`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/disk_full.rs#L46) | A full disk in the unpack sandbox stops unpacking. | 🆕 new |
| [`unpacking_stops_at_the_time_limit`](../crates/sam-intake/src/normalize.rs#L219) | Unpacking stops with a clear error when it takes longer than the time limit, so a huge or hostile AppImage cannot keep SAM busy forever. | ✅ |
| [`symlinks_pointing_outside_the_app_are_recognized`](../crates/sam-intake/src/normalize.rs#L233) | A symlink that points outside the unpacked app is recognized. | ✅ |
| [`paths_climbing_out_of_the_image_are_refused`](../crates/sam-intake/src/normalize.rs#L246) | Paths from the AppImage image are made relative, and paths that climb out with .. | ✅ |
| [`an_entry_named_dot_dot_writes_nothing_outside_the_app`](../crates/sam-intake/tests/climbing_paths.rs#L82) | An entry named dot dot writes nothing outside the app. | ✅ |
| [`unpacking_removes_setuid_bits`](../crates/sam-intake/src/normalize.rs#L257) | Unpacking removes setuid bits and makes files read-only for others. | ✅ |
| [`unpacking_stops_at_the_file_limit`](../crates/sam-intake/src/normalize.rs#L314) | Unpacking stops when the AppImage contains more files than the limit. | ✅ |
| [`a_built_appimage_is_detected_and_unpacked_safely`](../crates/sam-intake/src/testkit.rs#L288) | A built AppImage is recognized and unpacked, with setuid bits removed. | ✅ |
| [`size_limit_aborts_and_cleans_up`](../crates/sam-intake/src/testkit.rs#L347) | Unpacking stops at the size limit and leaves no partial files behind. | ✅ |
| [`fractions_follow_the_step_weights`](../crates/sam-runner/src/progress.rs#L152) | The progress bar follows the weights of the steps of creating a sandbox. | ✅ |
| [`bytes_written_are_counted`](../crates/sam-runner/src/progress.rs#L177) | Unpacking progress counts the bytes written. | ✅ |
| [`unpacking_stops_exactly_at_its_limits`](../crates/sam-intake/tests/limits.rs#L45) | The file-count and size limits let an app through that reaches them exactly and stop one entry or one byte more; a time limit of zero stops at once. | ✅ |
| [`only_an_exceeded_limit_counts_as_one`](../crates/sam-intake/src/extract.rs#L1312) | Only an exceeded limit counts as one: other errors are not explained as a limit the user could raise. | ✅ |
| [`the_unpacking_limits_hold_at_their_exact_edge`](../crates/sam-intake/src/extract.rs#L1348) | The counting holds the exact limits: as many bytes as allowed pass and one more is over; for the files it allows two entries of slack for the box's own folders (`normalize` holds the exact limit afterwards). | 🆕 new |
| [`folders_gone_or_unreadable_meanwhile_are_still_counted`](../crates/sam-intake/src/extract.rs#L1379) | A folder the tool removed or replaced meanwhile is passed over, and a folder the tool made unreadable inside the box is opened and counted: what it holds counts against the limits, never reported as "cannot be counted" and never passed over. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_folder_swapped_for_a_link_is_not_opened`](../crates/sam-intake/src/extract.rs#L1415) | A folder the tool swapped for a link while SAM counts is never opened through the link, wherever it leads, and a folder reached through a link further up leaves the box: nothing outside it is opened or changed. | 🆕 new |
| [`removing_a_missing_box_succeeds`](../crates/sam-intake/src/extract.rs#L1445) | Removing a box that is not there (never made, or removed already) is not an error. | 🆕 new |
| [`no_memory_limit_lets_the_tool_run`](../crates/sam-intake/src/extract.rs#L1466) | A memory limit of 0 means none: the tool runs. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_failed_tool_is_told_apart_from_a_limit_and_a_full_disk`](../crates/sam-intake/src/extract.rs#L1479) | How a tool's end is read: a plain failure is neither a limit nor a full disk; a full disk is told by the exit code or by the tool's own words, each on its own; a limit the tool reports comes with its reason. | 🆕 new |
| [`without_consent_the_appimages_own_unpacker_never_runs`](../crates/sam-intake/tests/damaged.rs#L25) | When SAM's own readers cannot unpack an AppImage, its own unpacker (code from the AppImage) runs only after the user agreed: without consent the unpacking stops and asks, and nothing of the AppImage runs. | ✅ |
| [`a_menu_start_after_free_space_unpacks_inside_a_sandbox`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/unpack.rs#L104) | A menu start after free space unpacks inside a sandbox. | ✅ |
| [`without_the_command_line_a_menu_start_refuses_to_unpack`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/unpack.rs#L127) | Without the command line a menu start refuses to unpack. | ✅ |
| [`sams_programs_never_unpack_without_a_sandbox`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/overview.rs#L590) | Sams programs never unpack without a sandbox. | ✅ |
| [`a_release_build_of_any_name_never_unpacks_without_a_sandbox`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/overview.rs#L617) | A release build of SAM refuses to unpack without a sandbox whatever its program is called, and the refusal names what is missing. | 🆕 new |
| [`sams_programs_refuse_without_a_sandbox_in_every_build`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/overview.rs#L662) | SAM's own programs refuse to unpack without a sandbox in every build, developer builds included. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_hostile_unpacking_tool_cannot_plant_links_in_sams_app_folder`](../crates/sam-intake/tests/hostile_extractor.rs#L32) | A hostile unpacking tool cannot plant links in sams app folder. | ✅ |
| [`nothing_in_the_unpack_sandbox_can_make_a_user_namespace`](../crates/sam-intake/tests/unpack_userns.rs#L47) | Nothing in the unpack sandbox can make a user namespace. | 🆕 new |
| [`files_sam_writes_never_go_through_a_planted_link`](../crates/sam-core/src/util.rs#L917) | SAM's own files in folders an untrusted program could have touched are written without following links: a planted link is replaced, the file it pointed to stays as it was, and a link in place of a folder becomes a real folder (audit I-F2). | ✅ |
| [`the_icon_is_cached_and_never_written_through_a_link`](../crates/sam-intake/src/metadata.rs#L325) | The app's icon is copied for SAM's menu entry and next to the unpacked app, replacing an older icon of another format, and never written through a link planted in the app's folder (audit I-F2). | ✅ |
| [`an_unpacking_limit_inside_the_sandbox_stops_unpacking`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/unpack.rs#L169) ⟲ | Unpacking limits hold inside the unpack sandbox too. | ✅ |
| [`a_named_pipe_is_refused_without_waiting`](../crates/sam-core/src/util.rs#L890) | Reading a file from an untrusted app returns at once for anything that is not a regular file: a named pipe made SAM wait forever (audit I-F9, I-F15). | ✅ |
| [`the_entry_from_exec_stays_inside_the_app`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/add.rs#L569) | An app without AppRun starts the program its menu entry names, found inside the app; a name that points outside it is never used. | ✅ |
| [`a_shared_unpack_folder_keeps_everything_that_is_not_sams`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/maintain.rs#L2443) | The unpack location can be any folder, for example `~/Documents`. | ✅ |
| [`a_menu_start_unpacks_in_a_sandbox_with_the_cli_next_to_it`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/overview.rs#L562) | A menu start unpacks in a sandbox with the cli next to it. | ✅ |
| [`tests_and_benchmarks_may_unpack_in_their_own_process`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/overview.rs#L679) | Tests and benchmarks may unpack in their own process. | ✅ |
| [`two_unpacks_at_once_get_their_own_error_files`](../crates/sam-intake/src/extract.rs#L1186) | Error files opened at the same moment in one process are separate files: an unpack running beside another no longer fails with "File exists" (it did on CI when two tests unpacked at once). | ✅ |
| [`add_with_progress_reports_from_the_first_step_to_the_end`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/commands.rs#L584) | `add --progress` prints the progress for the window as JSON lines on standard error: the first step and the end always. | 🆕 new |
| [`progress_lines_pass_new_steps_and_the_end_and_thin_out_the_rest`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/src/main.rs#L1723) | The progress lines: the first report of each step and the last one always pass, more reports of the same step only every 100 ms (the window's bar moves, and the per-file reports do not flood it). | 🆕 new |
| [`the_host_tools_unpack_what_sams_own_reader_cannot`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/unpack.rs#L335) | The host's unpacking tools unpack what SAM's own reader cannot, inside the unpack sandbox, with the options SAM gives them (contract unpack-tools; audit D-M10: its tests saw only SAM's own reader). | 🆕 new |

### 4. SAM reads the name, icon, description and update source from the app itself, and removes markup or control characters before showing them.

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`the_desktop_file_inside_an_appimage_is_read`](../crates/sam-intake/src/metadata.rs#L291) | The desktop file inside an AppImage is read with name, translations, categories and file types. | ✅ |
| [`reads_metadata_and_icon`](../crates/sam-intake/src/metadata.rs#L304) | Name, icon and categories are read from the unpacked app. | ✅ |
| [`a_readable_name_is_taken_from_the_file_name`](../crates/sam-intake/src/id.rs#L81) | A readable name is taken from an AppImage's file name, without version and architecture. | ✅ |
| [`icon_paths_cannot_climb_out_of_the_app`](../crates/sam-intake/src/peek.rs#L278) | Icon paths from the AppImage cannot climb out of the unpacked app. | ✅ |
| [`neutralizes_markup_and_controls`](../crates/sam-core/src/util.rs#L1289) | Text from an app is shown without markup or control characters, and only text longer than allowed is shortened. | ✅ |
| [`markup_in_metadata_is_neutralized`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/adversarial.rs#L95) | H3: markup in an AppImage's name or comment is neutralized before it is stored or shown. | ✅ |
| [`only_icons_that_are_safe_to_decode_are_taken`](../crates/sam-core/src/icon.rs#L131) | Only icons that are safe to decode reach the desktop's image code: a PNG whose header announces more than 1024×1024 pixels (a decode bomb: a small file that asks for gigabytes) is refused before anything decodes it, and so are oversized files, large SVGs and XPM (audit G-F8, I-F18). | ✅ |
| [`only_a_real_svg_counts_as_one`](../crates/sam-core/src/icon.rs#L171) ⟲ | Regression (review 2026-11-23, F3): any file with the text `<svg` in its first 2 KiB counted as an SVG, so an XPM, ICO or TGA icon carrying it in a comment or its pixels was installed as `.svg` and reached the desktop's decoder for its real format. | 🆕 new |
| [`preview_and_unpack_take_the_same_desktop_file`](../crates/sam-intake/src/metadata.rs#L265) | The preview and the unpacked app take the same desktop file: the top folder before `usr/share/applications`, each by name, and the first that leads to a file (audit I-F8: the preview gave up at a broken first one, the unpack took the next). | ✅ |
| [`changed_desktop_files_never_crash_the_reader`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/fuzz_targets.rs#L335) | Desktop files with a byte order mark, Windows line ends, odd sections and broken text are read without a panic, and the byte order mark and line ends do not hide the name. | ✅ |
| [`a_byte_order_mark_does_not_hide_the_desktop_entry`](../crates/sam-intake/src/metadata.rs#L254) | A desktop file that starts with a byte order mark keeps its keys (found by fuzzing: the mark hid the `[Desktop Entry]` line). | ✅ |

<a id="5"></a>
### 5. A damaged or hostile AppImage can't crash SAM or stop the list.

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`reading_a_file_of_an_appimage_is_bounded`](../crates/sam-core/src/util.rs#L1048) | A link to a device that never ends, or a file larger than the limit, cannot fill SAM's memory (regression: an AppImage whose AppRun links to /dev/zero made adding it grow without end). | ✅ |
| [`a_damaged_squashfs_image_gives_an_error_instead_of_a_crash`](../crates/sam-intake/tests/damaged.rs#L15) ⟲ | Regression: reading the name and icon of an AppImage whose SquashFS image makes the reader panic gives an error instead of crashing SAM. | ✅ |
| [`parsers_survive_garbage`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/adversarial.rs#L132) | Robustness: malformed desktop files and files that only look like AppImages must not crash the parsers (simple deterministic fuzzing). | ✅ |
| [`changed_appimages_never_crash_or_escape_the_unpacker`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/fuzz.rs#L141) | Changed AppImages are recognized or refused, and unpacked or refused, without a panic; unpacking writes only inside its own folder and keeps its time limit. | ✅ |
| [`changed_text_inputs_never_crash_the_parsers`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/fuzz.rs#L224) | Changed desktop files, zsync files and update information are parsed or refused without a panic. | ✅ |
| [`changed_programs_never_crash_the_analysis`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/fuzz.rs#L256) | Changed ELF programs inside an unpacked app are analyzed or skipped without a panic. | ✅ |
| [`one_damaged_appimage_does_not_stop_the_list`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/interface.rs#L299) ⟲ | Regression (found by the fuzzer): one damaged AppImage in a searched folder made `sam-jail discover` crash, so the window's list stayed empty. | ✅ |
| [`changed_signature_and_update_sections_never_crash_the_readers`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/fuzz_targets.rs#L117) | Changed ELF section tables and signature, key and update sections are read or refused without a panic: inspect, the signature check and the update information. | ✅ |
| [`the_preview_reader_panics_only_inside_the_squashfs_library`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/fuzz_targets.rs#L391) | The preview reader without its guard: a changed image must not make SAM's own code panic. | ✅ |
| [`impossible_header_values_give_no_elf_end`](../crates/sam-detect/src/appimage.rs#L343) | Header values that point past the end of every file give no ELF end, never an overflow: with overflow checks in release builds (audit P16) that would end SAM (audit I-F14, found again by fuzzing). | ✅ |
| [`a_dwarfs_image_after_the_elf_part_is_dwarfs`](../crates/sam-detect/src/appimage.rs#L377) | A DwarFS image right after the ELF part is taken as DwarFS, not as SquashFS: the SquashFS check looks for its own magic there. | ✅ |

## B. Choosing the right permissions

<a id="6"></a>
### 6. SAM recommends permissions from what the app is (window app, terminal tool, game, browser and so on), starting as strict as possible.

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`a_development_tool_gets_no_project_folder_on_its_own_word`](../crates/sam-profile/src/lib.rs#L925) | An app that calls itself a development tool gets Documents, but no writable project folder: the category is the AppImage's own claim, and a project folder lets an app plant code that runs outside the sandbox (git hooks, .envrc). | ✅ |
| [`terminal_programs_get_no_display`](../crates/sam-profile/src/lib.rs#L898) | Terminal programs get no display, GPU or desktop bus access. | ✅ |
| [`the_app_category_is_recognized_from_its_desktop_categories`](../crates/sam-profile/src/lib.rs#L955) | The category of an app is recognized from its desktop categories. | ✅ |
| [`recommendations_fit_the_category`](../crates/sam-profile/src/lib.rs#L1020) | Recommended permissions fit the category: a browser gets the network and Downloads, an editor no network. | ✅ |
| [`what_sam_finds_in_the_app_adjusts_the_recommendation`](../crates/sam-profile/src/lib.rs#L1035) | What SAM finds in the app (GPU, X11-only toolkit) adjusts the recommendation. | ✅ |
| [`bundled_libraries_are_sorted_by_what_they_reach`](../crates/sam-detect/src/classify.rs#L262) | Bundled libraries are sorted into system, graphics, display-controlled and dangerous classes. | ✅ |
| [`programs_that_load_graphics_at_run_time`](../crates/sam-detect/src/elf.rs#L1221) | Programs that load graphics libraries only at run time are recognized as needing the GPU. | ✅ |
| [`missing_optional_libraries_name_the_feature`](../crates/sam-detect/src/elf.rs#L1247) | A missing optional library is reported with the feature it provides, not as a broken app. | ✅ |
| [`programs_that_choose_the_qt_platform`](../crates/sam-detect/src/elf.rs#L1334) | Programs that choose the Qt platform themselves (X11 or Wayland) are recognized. | ✅ |
| [`qt_wayland_plugin_needs_its_client_library`](../crates/sam-detect/src/elf.rs#L1355) | A bundled Qt Wayland plugin counts as usable only when its Wayland client library is present. | ✅ |
| [`start_scripts_that_force_x11`](../crates/sam-detect/src/elf.rs#L1369) | Start scripts that force X11 are recognized, so SAM grants X11 to those apps. | ✅ |
| [`the_program_behind_a_start_script_is_found`](../crates/sam-detect/src/elf.rs#L1439) | When the AppImage starts through a shell script, SAM finds the program the script starts. | ✅ |
| [`an_appimage_asking_for_admin_rights_is_unsafe_to_sandbox`](../crates/sam-detect/src/elf.rs#L1461) | An AppImage that asks for administrator rights (pkexec) is marked as unsafe to sandbox. | ✅ |
| [`electron_markers_and_bundled_symlinks`](../crates/sam-detect/src/elf.rs#L1469) | Electron apps are recognized by their markers, and bundled library symlinks are followed. | ✅ |
| [`origin_relative_needed_paths`](../crates/sam-detect/src/elf.rs#L1482) | Libraries an app finds relative to its own folder ($ORIGIN) are counted as bundled, not as missing. | ✅ |
| [`the_c_library_version_an_app_needs_is_read`](../crates/sam-detect/src/elf.rs#L1498) | The C library version an app needs is read from its symbol versions. | ✅ |
| [`the_dependency_report_shows_what_is_missing_and_still_needed`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/src/output.rs#L664) | The dependency report shows the system libraries on one line, every other missing library on its own (NOT FOUND when the host lacks it), only what the user still has to grant, and the notes. | 🆕 new |
| [`redetect_shows_the_dependency_report`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/human_views.rs#L237) | `redetect` shows the dependency report it made. | 🆕 new |
| [`info_shows_comment_reasons_dependencies_and_warnings`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/src/output.rs#L692) | `info` shows the app's comment, why its profile is what it is, its dependency report and its warnings when it has them, and no empty sections when it has none. | 🆕 new |

<a id="7"></a>
### 7. SAM explains every permission in plain language and shows how risky it is.

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`the_strictest_window_profile_is_low_risk`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1178) | SAM's strictest window profile counts as low risk. | ✅ |
| [`full_home_is_dangerous`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1187) | Full home access counts as dangerous, and as think twice when it is read-only; its name says which, so a writable home never reads as a read-only one. | ✅ |
| [`network_and_x11_high`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1280) | Full network together with X11 makes the settings think twice (high risk). | ✅ |
| [`each_dangerous_setting_produces_its_warning`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L549) | Dangerous settings (X11, full home, system bus, network an app does not need) each produce a warning. | ✅ |
| [`strict_has_no_danger`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L639) | SAM's strictest window profile produces no danger warning. | ✅ |
| [`x11_warning_can_be_disabled`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L1493) | The X11 warning disappears when the user turns it off in the preferences. | ✅ |
| [`tags_and_the_risk_list_use_the_same_words`](../crates/sam-profile/src/tags.rs#L265) | One name per permission: where the risk list names a permission that also has a tag, both use the same words (regression: "Audio" vs "Sound", "GPU" vs "GPU acceleration", "Show in folder"). | ✅ |
| [`folder_explanations_name_the_path`](../crates/sam-gtk/src/all_perms.rs#L1508) | Personal folders explain where they are; other permissions keep their description unchanged. | ✅ |
| [`risk_has_the_same_three_words_everywhere`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L932) | The command line and the window name risk with the same words, and the command line's filter understands both names. | ✅ |
| [`the_preset_question_names_the_risk_in_its_words`](../crates/sam-gtk/src/window.rs#L1946) | The question before a preset names the risk before and after in the words the badges use ("Harmless", "Some access"), never the JSON's level names (owner decision 17 of 2026-11-23). | 🆕 new |
| [`presets_name_the_risk_in_its_words`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/human_views.rs#L201) | Saving and applying a preset name the risk in its words too: a preset that turns sound on reads "Some access" when it is saved, listed and applied, never the internal name "Medium" (owner decision 17 of 2026-11-23). | 🆕 new |
| [`the_risk_column_fits_the_widest_risk_word`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/src/output.rs#L468) | The risk columns of `list` and `preset list` are as wide as the widest risk word, so "Some access" (owner decision 17 of 2026-11-23) or a longer translation never pushes the next column out of line: every risk cell takes the same room on the screen. | 🆕 new |
| [`each_risk_level_has_words_of_its_own`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L945) | Each of the four levels has words of its own, and each word names exactly its level: `harmless` selects only Low, `some-access` (or "some access") only Medium (owner decision 17 of 2026-11-23). | 🆕 new |
| [`the_window_names_each_risk_level_in_its_own_words`](../crates/sam-gtk/src/util.rs#L527) | The window names each risk level with its own words, the same the command line uses: a Medium app reads "Some access", no longer "Harmless" like a Low one, in badges, the app page and the hover text (owner decision 17 of 2026-11-23). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_medium_app_reads_some_access_in_list_and_info`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/human_views.rs#L155) | An app rated Medium reads "Some access" in `list` and `info`, no longer "Harmless" like a Low one, and `--risk` selects it by these words only: `harmless` now selects Low apps alone (owner decision 17 of 2026-11-23). | 🆕 new |
| [`the_full_session_bus_is_rated_dangerous`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1166) | The full session bus reaches systemd, which can start programs outside the sandbox, so it is rated like allowing systemd by name. | ✅ |
| [`every_switch_has_one_risk_word`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L851) | Every switch says the same risk word in the window (its catalogue entry) as in the risk assessment that decides the badges and which changes ask first (audit D-M12: raw ALSA was "Think twice" in one and "Dangerous" in the other, so the window did not ask). | ✅ |
| [`switching_a_protection_off_counts`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1085) | Settings that take a protection away count in the risk word, and a secret passed through counts more than an ordinary variable (audit D-H4). | ✅ |
| [`reading_and_sending_together_is_dangerous`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1131) | The network together with your home, keys or passwords is Dangerous: each alone is Think twice, but together the app can send what it reads (audit U-M10). | ✅ |
| [`the_catalogue_names_every_setting`](../crates/sam-core/src/permissions.rs#L1328) | The catalogue is the one list of permissions: it names every setting an app's permissions have, and every name in it can be read. | ✅ |
| [`the_documentation_names_every_permission`](../crates/sam-core/src/permissions.rs#L1371) | The permissions page of the documentation names every permission. | ✅ |
| [`the_editor_shows_every_permission`](../crates/sam-gtk/src/perms.rs#L1300) | The editor (the app's page and the Permission Manager for one app) shows every permission in the catalogue, the one list of permissions (audit G-F14: the lists drifted; a setting was missing here). | ✅ |
| [`files_opened_with_the_app_offer_every_setting`](../crates/sam-gtk/src/perms.rs#L1314) | Files opened with the app offer every value the setting can hold: a stored "No access" showed as "Read-only", the first choice, since it had none of its own. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_no_access_choice_names_the_folders_the_app_sees_anyway`](../crates/sam-gtk/src/perms.rs#L1332) | The "No access" choice says what the sandbox keeps: a file inside a folder another permission shows stays visible through that permission, so the text may not promise that the app never sees an opened file (it did, while Documents set to Read-only still showed ~/Documents/report.pdf). | 🆕 new |
| [`info_shows_every_permission_with_its_value`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/src/output.rs#L370) | `info` shows every permission of the catalogue with its value, also the ones it left out before (audit G-F14). | ✅ |
| [`info_on_the_terminal_shows_every_permission_once`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/exit_codes.rs#L167) | `sam-jail info` shows every permission with its value, each group of permissions under one heading (audit G-F14). | ✅ |
| [`a_path_with_dots_or_double_slashes_is_rated_where_it_leads`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1330) | A path is rated where it leads, not as it is written: `.`, `..` and repeated `/` count for nothing, so `/.` is the whole computer and `~/..` the folder above the home, and a folder that holds the home counts as the home. | 🆕 new |
| [`an_extra_folder_that_leads_to_the_whole_computer_asks_first`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1364) | An extra folder written so that it mounts the whole computer, the whole home or a place found through `..` is Dangerous, warned about and asks first (review F1: `/.` read Harmless and a preset applied it without a question). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_grant_of_tmp_or_its_listening_places_is_dangerous_even_read_only`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1395) | A folder grant of /tmp, or of a place in it where outside programs listen, is Dangerous and warned about even read-only: connecting to a terminal session or an SSH connection there needs no write access (review SB-3). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_password_service_allowed_by_name_counts_as_the_keyring_switch`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1430) | A password service allowed by name counts as the keyring switch: Think twice, asks first, and with full network the app "can send what it reads". | 🆕 new |
| [`a_wildcard_over_the_portal_is_dangerous`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1494) | A wildcard that covers the portal's own name lets every portal call through, past SAM's choice of interfaces: Dangerous. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_sound_servers_own_socket_is_dangerous_as_an_extra_socket`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1557) | The sound server's own socket listed as an extra socket is as dangerous as "Sound without SAM's relay" (review SB-4); PipeWire's stays at the level of "Direct PipeWire access". | 🆕 new |
| [`a_function_of_a_password_service_counts_with_the_network`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1474) | A function rule of a password service reaches the stored passwords as the allowed name does: with full network the app "can send what it reads" (review 2026-11-23). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_dangerous_socket_is_known_however_it_is_written`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1570) | A dangerous socket is known however its path is written: `.` and doubled `/` count for nothing, as for the kernel (review 2026-11-23: `/run/dbus//system_bus_socket` read as an ordinary extra socket). | 🆕 new |
| [`key_agents_are_rated_dangerous`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1604) | Key agents are Dangerous: whoever reaches one uses your unlocked keys to decrypt, sign or log in to other computers as you (owner decision 11 of 2026-11-23). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_key_agent_socket_with_the_network_can_send_what_it_reads`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1659) | An extra socket that is a key agent counts as reading your keys: with the full network, the app can send what the agent decrypts (owner decision 11 of 2026-11-23). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_terminal_that_runs_commands_is_dangerous_to_reach`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1514) | A terminal that runs any command it is sent is Dangerous to reach, by name, a wildcard over it or a function rule, so adding it asks first (audit D-M6: `set APP dbus.session.allow org.gnome.Terminal` added no risk and asked nothing). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_presets_risk_word_comes_from_its_permissions`](../crates/sam-storage/src/lib.rs#L1397) | The risk word of a preset is worked out from its permissions, never taken from the file: a file placed in the presets folder that says "low" for full home and network is listed as Dangerous (review F6). | 🆕 new |
| [`every_state_risk_and_severity_is_shown_in_its_own_words`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/src/output.rs#L434) | Every state, risk level and warning severity is shown in its own words: `list`, `info`, `add`, `set` and `evaluate` tell the user an app is Unsafe, Dangerous or lacks its protection only through these. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_risk_line_names_the_level_and_what_sets_it`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/src/output.rs#L486) | The risk line names the level and the permissions that set it. | 🆕 new |
| [`every_warning_is_shown_with_its_severity_title_and_message`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/src/output.rs#L504) | Every warning is shown with its severity, title and message. | 🆕 new |
| [`evaluate_and_info_show_the_risk_and_every_warning`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/human_views.rs#L106) | `evaluate` and `info` show the risk with what sets it and every warning, as their JSON has them. | 🆕 new |
| [`info_shows_comment_reasons_dependencies_and_warnings`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/src/output.rs#L692) | `info` shows the app's comment, why its profile is what it is, its dependency report and its warnings when it has them, and no empty sections when it has none. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_reason_names_exactly_the_riskiest_permissions`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1219) | The reason given for a risk word names exactly the permissions at that level: the riskiest ones, never the milder ones next to them and never the basics every sandbox gets; low risk names none. | 🆕 new |
| [`game_controllers_count_unless_all_input_is_granted`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1245) | Game controllers count on their own; with all input devices granted they are part of that one, which counts as dangerous. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_risk_level_shows_as_its_name`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1266) | A level shows as its name where it is put into a text (the message after saving a preset). | 🆕 new |
| [`each_credential_store_in_a_full_home_is_named_as_exposed_keys`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L685) | With the whole home granted, each credential store on its own (SSH keys, GnuPG keys, the other hidden files) gives the danger warning that private keys are visible; with all three off there is none. | 🆕 new |
| [`only_the_full_session_bus_is_named_as_an_escape`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L710) | Only an unfiltered session bus is named as a way out of the sandbox; a filtered bus or none is not. | 🆕 new |
| [`dangerous_bus_names_and_a_missing_proxy_are_named_for_the_filtered_bus_only`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L731) | On a filtered session bus, an allowed name that reaches outside the sandbox gives a danger warning, and a missing xdg-dbus-proxy is named. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_wildcard_over_a_blocked_name_is_named_for_the_filtered_bus_only`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L760) | A wildcard that also covers a blocked name is named only when the filtered bus would use it: not for a filter without such a wildcard, and not for a full or no session bus, where the lists do not apply. | 🆕 new |
| [`each_raw_device_gives_the_raw_device_danger`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L792) | Raw input devices (keystrokes), raw USB and raw sound devices each give the danger warning on their own; the strict profile none. | 🆕 new |
| [`raw_backend_arguments_are_named_when_there_are_some`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L814) | Unchecked Bubblewrap arguments are named when there are some, and only then. | 🆕 new |
| [`no_display_is_named_only_without_any_display`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L826) | "Cannot open windows" is said only with neither Wayland nor X11; a terminal program gets its own notice instead. | 🆕 new |
| [`network_advice_fits_the_setting_and_the_apps_need`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L859) | The network advice fits both the setting and what the app seems to need: "may not be needed" only for network on and not needed, "network is off" only for network off and needed. | 🆕 new |
| [`unusable_user_namespaces_are_named_only_where_they_are`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L893) | Unusable user namespaces are a danger notice on such a computer only; setuid Bubblewrap makes them usable again. | 🆕 new |
| [`missing_display_and_sound_are_named_only_when_the_app_would_miss_them`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L921) | A missing Wayland socket, X11 display or sound server is named only when the app uses it, the host lacks it and no fallback is there. | 🆕 new |
| [`missing_sound_names_pipewire_pulse_when_pipewire_runs`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L1049) | Without pipewire-pulse the sound notice names it when PipeWire runs. | 🆕 new |
| [`every_grant_the_risk_words_question_is_warned_about`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L574) ⟲ | Regression (audit U-M10): grants the risk words rate "Think twice" had no warning at all, the private-keys warning was silenced by switching off the full-home warning, and the full session bus was a plain warning while the risk words call it Dangerous. | 🆕 new |
| [`turning_a_protection_off_for_every_app_asks_first`](../crates/sam-gtk/src/pages.rs#L1217) | Turning Landlock or the syscall filter off for every app asks first; turning them on, and every other preference, does not (audit G-F11). | 🆕 new |
| [`the_window_confirms_turning_a_protection_off_exactly_when_it_asked`](../crates/sam-gtk/src/pages.rs#L1244) | The window asks before exactly the switches sam-core's rule names (the ones `config set` refuses without `--yes`), and confirms the change for the command line exactly when it asked: without the `--yes` the command line refuses it, with one it never asked about the window would turn a protection off unasked. | 🆕 new |
| [`turning_a_protection_off_for_every_app_needs_yes`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/commands.rs#L765) | `config set` turns Landlock or the syscall filter off for every app only with --yes, as the window asks first: without it the change is refused with `config-needs-yes`, names what goes off and nothing is saved. | 🆕 new |
| [`putting_a_configuration_that_turns_a_protection_off_needs_yes`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/commands.rs#L798) | `config put` saves a configuration that turns the syscall filter off for every app only with --yes (code `config-needs-yes`, nothing saved); one that changes something else needs none. | 🆕 new |

**Code:** [`sam-core/src/permissions.rs`](../crates/sam-core/src/permissions.rs), [`sam-core/src/risk.rs`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs), [`sam-core/src/warnings.rs`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs), [`sam-gtk/src/all_perms.rs`](../crates/sam-gtk/src/all_perms.rs), [`sam-jail-cli/src/output.rs`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/src/output.rs)

<a id="8"></a>
### 8. You can change permissions per app, for several apps at once, or through your own presets.

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`set_and_get_keys`](../crates/sam-core/src/permissions.rs#L1476) | Permissions can be read and set by key, and unknown keys or values are refused. | ✅ |
| [`an_empty_list_left_out_of_the_saved_form_takes_comma_separated_input`](../crates/sam-core/src/permissions.rs#L1513) | A list the saved form leaves out while it is empty (function and signal rules) takes comma-separated input like every other list, also while it is still empty. | 🆕 new |
| [`change_check_flags_removals_and_additions`](../crates/sam-profile/src/lib.rs#L1086) | The change check names what an app would lose that it needs, and the dangerous access it would gain. | ✅ |
| [`folders_are_not_breakage_but_lost_local_network_and_bus_are`](../crates/sam-profile/src/lib.rs#L1150) | A hidden folder is not a reason to warn that an app stops working: it is listed apart (regression for the group warning, which only interrupts for breakage). | ✅ |
| [`lost_sound_is_named_sound`](../crates/sam-profile/src/lib.rs#L1180) | An app that needs sound is warned about in the switch's word. | ✅ |
| [`preset_template_is_strict`](../crates/sam-profile/src/lib.rs#L1198) | A new preset starts without home, network or system bus access. | ✅ |
| [`only_a_damaged_preset_is_moved_aside`](../crates/sam-storage/src/lib.rs#L1342) | A damaged preset is moved aside once; a preset from a newer SAM is only skipped and stays where it is, for when SAM is updated (mutation run P14: moving every unreadable preset aside went unnoticed). | ✅ |
| [`user_changes_survive_better_recommendation`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/mod.rs#L830) | When SAM's recommendation improves, the user's own changes are kept. | ✅ |
| [`a_dry_run_changes_nothing_and_names_what_would_stop_working`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/groups.rs#L56) | A dry run of a group change saves nothing and names what each app would lose. | ✅ |
| [`only_changed_apps_are_saved_and_one_undo_restores_all_of_them`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/groups.rs#L82) | A group change saves only the apps whose value changes, and one undo restores all of them. | ✅ |
| [`an_invalid_value_or_unknown_app_changes_no_app`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/groups.rs#L118) | A group change with an invalid value or an unknown app changes no app. | ✅ |
| [`sam_ships_no_presets_and_old_built_in_names_are_free_for_the_user`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/groups.rs#L157) | SAM ships no presets; old built-in names are free for the user, and applying a preset asks before it removes something an app needs. | ✅ |
| [`saving_an_apps_settings_as_a_preset_keeps_them_exactly`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/groups.rs#L235) | Saving an app's settings as a preset keeps them exactly. | ✅ |
| [`reset_to_recommendation_brings_back_the_recommended_settings`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/settings.rs#L147) | "Reset to recommendation" brings back SAM's recommended settings and turns compatibility mode off. | ✅ |
| [`preset_names_cannot_place_files_outside_the_presets_folder`](../crates/sam-storage/src/lib.rs#L1141) | A preset is always stored inside the presets folder, whatever its name: names like "../../evil" or "/etc/x" (typed, or from an imported file) cannot place a file elsewhere. | ✅ |
| [`a_saved_preset_loads_back_unchanged`](../crates/sam-storage/src/lib.rs#L1467) | A saved preset loads back unchanged, found by its name in any letter case. | ✅ |
| [`group_list_set_and_restore_round_trip`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/interface.rs#L79) | The JSON interface lists apps with their tags, changes a permission for several apps, and restores them with the returned "previous" object. | ✅ |
| [`group_set_refuses_bad_input_without_changing_anything`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/interface.rs#L236) | A group change with an invalid value, an unknown key or no app is refused, and no app changes. | ✅ |
| [`presets_are_the_users_own_only`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/interface.rs#L259) | The command line lists only the user's own presets; the template is strict, and saved presets carry no built-in flag. | ✅ |
| [`each_permission_adds_exactly_its_part_to_the_command`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4269) | Each granted permission adds exactly its part to the Bubblewrap command. | ✅ |
| [`only_new_or_higher_serious_risks_ask`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L981) | The one ask rule: a change asks when it adds something rated Think twice or Dangerous that was not there, or raises it; taking a risk away, keeping it, or adding something harmless does not ask (audit G-F2). | ✅ |
| [`a_change_waits_for_a_yes_by_one_rule`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1056) | The one rule for a yes: Think twice and Dangerous wait for one, Some access and Harmless do not; compatibility mode and a change from what the app printed wait for one whatever their level (audit D-H5, U-M2). | 🆕 new |
| [`one_gate_refuses_each_change_that_waits_with_its_own_code`](../crates/sam-runner/src/gate.rs#L281) | One gate for every change: a change of settings that adds Think twice or Dangerous, also for several apps at once (each item named once), compatibility mode turned on, a protection turned off for every app and a step from what the app printed are each refused with their own code until the person says yes; a Some access change, turning compatibility mode off and turning a protection on go through unasked. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_change_from_an_old_copy_keeps_what_changed_meanwhile`](../crates/sam-profile/src/lib.rs#L826) | A change made on an old copy applies only what it changed: another setting changed meanwhile stays; the same setting changed elsewhere to another value is named, not overwritten (audit G-F4). | ✅ |
| [`a_change_from_the_window_keeps_changes_made_elsewhere`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/interface.rs#L208) | `profile change` applies only what differs between "from" and "to", as the window sends it: a change made elsewhere since the window loaded the settings stays (audit G-F4). | ✅ |
| [`undo_takes_back_only_its_own_change`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/interface.rs#L158) | Undo takes back only its own change: a change made to an app since stays, and a later change to the same setting refuses the whole undo instead of being overwritten (audit G-F4: the whole sets from before were written back). | ✅ |
| [`a_preset_not_applied_ends_with_not_passed`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/exit_codes.rs#L210) | A preset that is not applied (it needs a confirmation) ends with 3 and still says what it would change; applied, it ends with 0. | ✅ |
| [`presets_keep_their_own_files_and_any_name`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/odd_names.rs#L173) | Two presets whose names share a file ("My Preset" and "my-preset") never overwrite each other without a word (audit G-F19), and a preset called `-x` is a name like any other when it comes after `--`, as the window passes it (G-F20). | ✅ |
| [`the_last_rule_and_a_variable_can_be_taken_away`](../crates/sam-profile/src/lib.rs#L869) | The last function rule, the last signal rule and a set variable can be taken away through a change (the window's switches, the undo of a group change): the saved form leaves empty rule lists out, and a removed variable is missing from the map, which used to come back as null and refuse the whole change (review F3: a Dangerous rule to systemd could not be taken back from the window). | 🆕 new |
| [`every_setting_can_be_set_by_name_also_while_left_out`](../crates/sam-core/src/permissions.rs#L1763) | The function and signal rules can be set by name while they are empty, as a comma list like every other list (review F8: the saved form leaves them out while empty, and `sam-jail set` and `group set` refused them), and every setting of the catalogue can be set to the value it reads. | 🆕 new |
| [`an_imported_preset_never_replaces_one_of_the_same_name`](../crates/sam-storage/src/lib.rs#L1426) | Importing a preset file never replaces a preset of the same name: it gets a free name instead, and the person's own stays as it was (review F9). | 🆕 new |
| [`set_shows_the_warnings_about_the_changed_setting`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/commands.rs#L541) | `set` on the terminal shows the warnings about the setting just changed, and only those. | 🆕 new |
| [`an_apps_own_name_never_captures_a_reference_to_another_app`](../crates/sam-storage/src/lib.rs#L1305) | An AppImage chooses its own name: an app named `vlc` does not capture a command meant for "VLC media player", which `vlc` also names; the reference is refused as naming more than one app, and each app is still found by its ID or a reference only it matches (audit G-F7: the exact name won, so `sam-jail set vlc network.mode full` changed the hostile app without a word). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_preset_changed_after_the_question_is_not_applied`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/groups.rs#L196) | The window asks about a preset, then applies it by name: a preset changed while the question was open is refused, and only the preset as it was asked about is applied (audit G-F12/13: the changed one was applied unseen). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_changed_switch_marks_the_profile_as_changed_and_undoing_it_clears_the_mark`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/profiles.rs#L779) | The mark that the person changed an app's settings, which `sam profile get` shows, says whether they differ from SAM's recommendation after each change: set when a switch leaves it, and cleared when the switch goes back. | 🆕 new |

**Code:** [`sam-core/src/permissions.rs`](../crates/sam-core/src/permissions.rs), [`sam-profile/src/lib.rs`](../crates/sam-profile/src/lib.rs), [`sam-runner/src/ops/profiles.rs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/profiles.rs), [`sam-runner/src/gate.rs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/gate.rs), [`sam-storage/src/lib.rs`](../crates/sam-storage/src/lib.rs)

**Also tried by:** [`tests/gui/permissions-atspi.sh`](../tests/gui/permissions-atspi.sh)

<a id="9"></a>
### 9. Things an app printed can never grant it access on their own; SAM always asks.

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`app_output_cannot_buy_dangerous_grants`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/adversarial.rs#L74) | H2/M4: suggestions derived from app output never grant escape-capable services or protected folders, and are always marked as app-derived. | ✅ |
| [`a_forged_trace_line_cannot_widen_a_low_risk_grant`](../crates/sam-runner/src/diagnose.rs#L600) | A forged trace line cannot widen a low-risk grant: paths with ".." are ignored, and a grant is only ever a real folder of shared system data, never a parent such as /usr. | ✅ |
| [`an_app_nobody_has_confirmed_does_not_start`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/start_check.rs#L131) | An app added on the command line without `--accept` has settings nobody has confirmed: SAM chose them partly from what the app says about itself (its name and description pick its category, and with it the network). | ✅ |
| [`a_folder_or_password_store_from_the_apps_words_is_judged_where_it_leads`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs#L1171) | A folder the app names in its output is judged where it leads: with `..` it can name `/tmp`, another home or the whole computer while it still starts with the home, so it is never offered. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_apps_own_words_never_give_it_a_terminal_to_talk_to`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs#L1027) | What the app prints never gets it a terminal to talk to either: a terminal runs any command it is sent, outside the sandbox (audit D-M6, the TALK half: "Allow just this service" was offered as harmless). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_step_the_apps_output_raised_stays_its_request_under_a_hints_name`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/commands.rs#L318) | A step the app's own output raised stays the app's request when SAM has a first-run hint of the same name. | 🆕 new |

**Code:** [`sam-runner/src/repair.rs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs)

## C. The sandbox

<a id="10"></a>
### 10. The app sees only a private home folder and the folders you grant.

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`links_in_etc_never_bring_sockets_or_devices`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L3896) | A link in /etc never brings a socket or a device into the sandbox, wherever it points; plain files and folders of the system's data and settings do, and in /run only plain files (regression: Debian's `/etc/alternatives/php-fpm.sock` showed the PHP server's socket). | ✅ |
| [`a_user_folder_that_shows_more_is_not_shown`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L6242) | A user folder set to the root, the folder above the home, a link to it, a key folder or SAM's folder is never shown; a usual one is, at its real place with a link where the app looks (audit S-F11). | ✅ |
| [`a_user_folder_above_a_linked_home_is_not_shown`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L2173) | A user folder is refused by each of the rules on its own, also when the home is kept behind a link (`~` at `home/ann`, a link to `data/ann`), where the home as named and the home where it really is answer differently: - the folder that holds the home's link (`home`) lies above the home as named, not above the real home: shown, it handed the app the link and every other home there; - the key folder by the home's linked name (`~/.ssh`) is a key store of the real home only; - a folder in `/tmp` where programs outside the sandbox listen (`/tmp`, `/tmp/tmux-1000`) is neither above the home nor a sensitive place, yet hands over the host's terminal multiplexers. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_writable_folder_stays_writable_in_a_read_only_home`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L3863) | With the home read-only, a folder set to read/write stays writable; one set to read-only adds nothing (the home shows it read-only). | ✅ |
| [`a_user_folder_is_mounted_at_its_own_access_with_the_whole_home`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L7124) | With the whole home granted, a user folder is mounted at its own access wherever the home does not already show it at exactly that access: read-only in a writable home (finding R63), writable in a read-only one, and outside the home at all (`XDG_DOCUMENTS_DIR=/data/Documents`), read-only or writable. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_read_only_user_folder_under_a_writable_home_is_read_only`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/matrix.rs#L899) | With the whole home writable, Documents set to read-only is read-only inside a real sandbox: the app can read it but neither change a file there, add one nor rename the folder away, and the files on the host stay as they were, while the rest of the home stays writable. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_read_only_user_folder_outside_the_home_is_shown_with_the_whole_home`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/sandbox.rs#L427) | A user folder outside the home (`XDG_DOCUMENTS_DIR=/data/Documents`) is shown at its own access with the whole home granted, read-only or writable, the home read-only or writable: inside a real sandbox the app reads it, writes there only when it is set to read and write, and a read-only one stays as it was on the host. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_user_folder_behind_a_link_the_app_could_plant_is_not_shown`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L7352) | A user folder reached through a link the app could have put there is not shown where the link leads: with the whole home writable, `mv ~/Downloads ~/D; ln -s /data/elsewhere ~/Downloads` in one start showed `/data/elsewhere` writable in the next, any place on the computer the app chose. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_user_folder_link_the_app_planted_never_shows_where_it_leads`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/sandbox.rs#L527) | An app with the whole home writable and Downloads granted turns `~/Downloads` into a link to a folder outside the home that it was never shown (in one start). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_user_folder_link_the_app_planted_outside_tmp_never_shows_where_it_leads`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/sandbox.rs#L801) | [`a_user_folder_link_the_app_planted_never_shows_where_it_leads`] with the place the link leads to outside `/tmp`, so the rule for links the app could have planted keeps it out, not the rule for places where programs outside the sandbox listen: the app can neither read nor write there, and the start says the app may have made that link. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_user_folder_where_outside_programs_listen_is_not_shown`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L7472) | A user folder set to `/tmp` or to a folder in it where programs outside the sandbox listen (`/tmp/tmux-1000`) is not shown, with or without the whole home: bound over the sandbox's own `/tmp`, it handed the app the host's terminal multiplexers and SSH agents, read-only too (`tmux -S /tmp/tmux-1000/default new-window …` runs outside the sandbox). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_write_grant_inside_a_read_only_user_folder_stays_writable`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L7515) | An extra folder granted read and write inside a read-only user folder under the writable whole home (`~/Documents/proj`, Documents read-only) is bound writable after the read-only folder, as the switch says and the sandbox check expects: the app cannot change its way, since the read-only folder is a mount of its own. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_writable_extra_folder_inside_a_read_only_user_folder_stays_writable`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/matrix.rs#L1002) | With the whole home writable and Documents read-only, an extra folder granted read and write inside Documents (`~/Documents/proj`) is writable inside a real sandbox, while Documents around it stays read-only. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_link_inside_a_read_only_folder_in_the_writable_home_is_not_followed`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L7574) | A link inside a read-only folder under the writable whole home counts as one the app could have put there: such a folder is not mounted at all when the sandbox refuses it (Documents itself a link to `~/Dropbox/Documents` in the writable home), so the home shows it writable; and where it is mounted, the app may have written there in an earlier start, before it was set read-only. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_link_planted_inside_a_refused_read_only_folder_is_never_followed`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/sandbox.rs#L710) | With the whole home writable, Documents read-only but itself a link in the home (`~/Documents` to `~/Dropbox/Documents`, so SAM does not mount it) and Downloads read and write inside it, an app turns Downloads into a link to a folder outside the home that it was never shown. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_link_planted_inside_a_refused_read_only_folder_outside_tmp_is_never_followed`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/sandbox.rs#L852) | [`a_link_planted_inside_a_refused_read_only_folder_is_never_followed`] with the place the link leads to outside `/tmp`, so the rule for links the app could have planted keeps it out, not the rule for places where programs outside the sandbox listen: the app can neither read nor write there, and the start says the app may have made that link. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_folders_above_a_folder_bound_at_its_own_access_are_pinned`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L7670) | The folders above a folder bound at an access of its own inside a folder the app can change are pinned: above a read-only user folder (`~/Sync/Documents` read-only under the writable home), above a read-only extra folder (`~/work/ref`), and so above a writable extra folder inside a read-only one (`~/Sync/Documents/proj`), with or without the whole home. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_folders_above_a_read_only_user_folder_cannot_be_renamed`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/sandbox.rs#L911) | With the whole home writable, Documents read-only in `~/Sync`, an extra writable folder inside it and an extra read-only folder in `~/work`, the app can rename neither `~/Sync` nor `~/work`: each is a mount point of its own. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_folder_bound_read_only_for_the_fonts_is_never_pinned`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L7855) | The folders SAM always binds read-only (the fonts and the font settings) are never pinned where their read-only bind is still the topmost mount, on them or inside them: with the whole home writable and an extra read-only folder inside a font folder (`~/.fonts/licensed`), a pin of `~/.fonts` was bound after its read-only bind (equal depth, stable order) and made the font folder writable, which the start never allowed before the pins of finding R63. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_font_folders_stay_read_only_around_an_extra_folder_inside`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/sandbox.rs#L952) | With the whole home writable and an extra read-only folder inside each font folder (`~/.fonts/licensed`, `~/.local/share/fonts/a/licensed`), the font folders stay read-only, as SAM always binds them: the pins of the folders above a read-only extra folder (review of finding R63) once bound `~/.fonts` writable after its read-only bind. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_writable_folder_inside_a_read_only_font_or_theme_place_keeps_its_pins`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L7994) | A writable extra folder inside a place SAM binds read-only for the fonts or the themes, or at one, is bound after it and writable: a start file kept as a link into it (`~/.bashrc` to `~/.fonts/work/dots/bashrc`) keeps the pins of the folders above where its guard lands (review B2 S3, R50), so the app cannot rename `dots` away with the guard. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_linked_start_file_inside_a_writable_folder_in_the_fonts_stays_pinned`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/sandbox.rs#L990) | With the whole home writable, `~/.fonts` an extra read-only folder, an extra writable folder inside it (`~/.fonts/work`) and `~/.bashrc` kept as a link to `~/.fonts/work/dots/bashrc`, the app cannot rename `dots`: it is a mount point of its own, so the guard of the start file stays where the link leads (review B2 S3, R50). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_writable_user_folder_the_home_shows_inside_a_font_folder_adds_no_pin`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L8110) | A writable user folder inside a font folder under the writable whole home (`XDG_MUSIC_DIR=~/.fonts/work`) gets no mount of its own, since the home already shows it writable, so the read-only bind of `~/.fonts` stays the topmost mount there. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_font_folder_opened_or_bound_writable_keeps_the_pins_inside`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L8567) | A font folder opened with the app, or bound writable by SAM itself, is bound writable after its read-only bind at its own place, so it is writable and the folders inside it are pinned as everywhere: the folder above an extra read-only folder inside it (`~/.fonts/dots/licensed`) cannot be renamed away with that mount. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_place_bound_writable_elsewhere_leaves_the_font_folders_unpinned`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L7908) | A place bound writable somewhere else (an extra writable folder, a file opened writable, a writable bind of SAM's, each outside the home) does not cover the fonts and the font settings: their read-only bind is still the topmost mount, so they are never pinned writable, as in a start without it. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_writable_user_folder_at_a_font_folder_adds_no_pin_inside`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L8457) | A writable user folder set to a font folder itself (`XDG_MUSIC_DIR=~/.fonts`), without the whole home or with the home read-only, gets its own writable mount at the same place as the font folder's read-only bind. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_writable_user_folder_inside_a_font_folder_in_a_private_home_keeps_its_pins`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L8505) | A writable user folder inside a font folder in a start without the whole home (`XDG_MUSIC_DIR=~/.fonts/work`) gets its own writable mount, after the read-only bind of `~/.fonts`, so the app can rename what lies inside it. | 🆕 new |
| [`user_folders_at_one_place_take_the_stricter_access`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L7769) | Two user folders set to one place with different access (Music read and write, Pictures read-only, both `~/Media`) take the stricter one, whatever their order in `XDG_DIR_KEYS`: `~/Media` is read-only, in the mounts as in the views, with the whole home writable, read-only or not granted, and the start names the folders and the rule (owner decision of 2026-11-27). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_user_folder_the_sandbox_does_not_show_is_named_before_the_start`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L1285) | A granted user folder the sandbox does not show is named on the app page and in `sam-jail info` before the start, with the launch note's words and the way out: Downloads set to `/tmp` (so downloads clear themselves), where programs outside the sandbox listen, and one reached through a link in the writable home. | 🆕 new |
| [`user_folders_at_one_place_with_different_access_are_named`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L1430) | Two granted user folders set to one place at different access (Music read and write, Pictures read-only, both `~/Media`) are named on the app page and in `sam-jail info` before the start, with the place, the rule (the stricter setting wins, so the app sees it read-only) and the way out, at the switch of the writable one, whatever their order. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_strictest_profile_shares_nothing_and_keeps_the_app_read_only`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4162) | The strictest profile builds a sandbox without network, environment or capabilities, with the app read-only. | ✅ |
| [`files_and_folders_named_on_the_command_line`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4064) | Files and folders named when starting an app are passed in, but never sensitive folders like autostart. | ✅ |
| [`files_opened_with_the_app_offer_every_setting`](../crates/sam-gtk/src/perms.rs#L1314) | Files opened with the app offer every value the setting can hold: a stored "No access" showed as "Read-only", the first choice, since it had none of its own. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_no_access_choice_names_the_folders_the_app_sees_anyway`](../crates/sam-gtk/src/perms.rs#L1332) | The "No access" choice says what the sandbox keeps: a file inside a folder another permission shows stays visible through that permission, so the text may not promise that the app never sees an opened file (it did, while Documents set to Read-only still showed ~/Documents/report.pdf). | 🆕 new |
| [`the_loaders_extra_library_folders_are_shown_read_only`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4024) | Extra library folders of the system loader (for example CUDA) are visible to the app read-only. | ✅ |
| [`each_permission_adds_exactly_its_part_to_the_command`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4269) | Each granted permission adds exactly its part to the Bubblewrap command. | ✅ |
| [`a_real_sandbox_shows_exactly_what_each_profile_allows`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/sandbox.rs#L68) | Inside a real sandbox, each profile shows exactly the files and services it allows. | ✅ |
| [`each_permission_grants_exactly_its_access_in_a_real_sandbox`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/matrix.rs#L169) | Inside a real sandbox, each permission grants exactly its access and nothing more. | ✅ |
| [`only_proper_variable_names_are_set`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L8651) | Only proper variable names are set inside the sandbox, and no value with a NUL byte. | ✅ |
| [`user_folders_come_from_user_dirs_dirs`](../crates/sam-core/src/host.rs#L1047) | The user folders come from user-dirs.dirs, with the usual names as fallbacks; a folder set to $HOME is disabled (contract xdg-folders). | ✅ |
| [`links_beside_a_shown_system_path_bring_only_allowed_targets`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L8949) | Next to a shown system path, links to the system's data and settings are shown too (`/usr/share/X11/xkb` → `xkeyboard-config-2`), once; links to anywhere else are not, and neither are links that stay inside the path (shown already). | 🆕 new |
| [`top_level_system_paths_are_copied_as_they_are`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L8987) | A top-level path of the system is copied as it is on the host: a link stays a link, a real folder is shown read-only, and anything else (a file, nothing) is left out. | 🆕 new |
| [`empty_arguments_and_options_open_nothing`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9011) | An empty argument (the folder the app was started in) and options are never taken as files to share, even where a file of that name exists. | 🆕 new |
| [`host_themes_are_shown_only_when_asked_for_without_the_home`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9051) | The host's theme settings in the home are shown, read-only, only when asked for and the home is not shown anyway. | 🆕 new |
| [`theme_private_files_are_hidden_only_when_the_theme_settings_are_shown`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9084) | The file chooser's bookmarks and servers in the theme folders are hidden (an empty read-only file in their place) only where SAM shows the theme settings on its own, with no home shown. | 🆕 new |
| [`ordinary_font_folders_and_links_inside_the_home_still_work`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/planted_bind_links.rs#L457) | The controls: fonts folders and links that stay inside what the app is shown still work. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_start_folder_reached_through_a_link_is_kept`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9330) | An app started in a folder it reaches only through a link (`~/link` to a granted `/data/proj`) starts there, so relative names work. | 🆕 new |
| [`every_user_folder_switch_grants_exactly_its_folder`](../crates/sam-core/src/permissions.rs#L1539) | Each user-folder switch grants exactly its own folder, read-only or writable, and no other. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_user_folders_are_read_from_the_users_config_folder`](../crates/sam-core/src/host.rs#L1021) | `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` decides where user-dirs.dirs is read, so which real folder a Documents or Downloads permission shows; an empty one counts as unset, never as the current folder. | 🆕 new |
| [`only_library_folders_outside_the_home_and_system_areas_are_shown`](../crates/sam-core/src/host.rs#L1099) | Only library folders a sandbox does not show anyway and that may be shown are kept: below /opt or /usr/local, never below the home folder (also where the home is itself below /opt) or in /etc, /var or /run. | 🆕 new |
| [`library_folders_are_judged_by_where_they_lead`](../crates/sam-core/src/host.rs#L1135) | A library folder is judged by its name and by where it leads: a name with `..` or a link below /opt that leads into a system area or the home, or a folder whose real place is unknown, is not shown (review B2 R60). | 🆕 new |
| [`library_folders_follow_real_links`](../crates/sam-core/src/host.rs#L1169) | The same on a real file system, with the real lookup: a root that leads elsewhere (Fedora Atomic's `/opt` to `/var/opt`) still shows its folders; a link out of a root, into the home (a home below /opt, known by its linked name) or through a root that leads to `/` does not. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_loaders_folders_come_from_its_config_files`](../crates/sam-core/src/host.rs#L1205) | The loader's folders come from the files in ld.so.conf.d in the order of their names, after /usr/local/lib: only lines that name a folder, and only folders that exist. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_loaders_folders_on_this_computer_are_found`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/host_probe.rs#L193) | The loader's library folders SAM shows are this computer's: every existing folder its ld.so.conf.d files list, and /usr/local/lib, which is outside the folders a sandbox shows anyway. | 🆕 new |
| [`an_app_is_never_told_a_folder_it_cannot_see`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/launch_hygiene.rs#L282) | Audit L-F16: started from a folder the app cannot see, the app is not told that folder's name as `$OWD`: it gets the folder it really starts in, its home. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_terminal_apps_start_line_names_only_the_folders_it_sees`](../crates/sam-runner/src/launch.rs#L2939) | A terminal app's start line names the extra folders the sandbox shows, never one inside SAM's own folders, which the sandbox leaves out wherever SAM keeps them (SAM's data in `~/Games/sam`): read-only or writable, it was listed as seen (independent review of decision 9). | 🆕 new |
| [`private_folders_and_logs_are_for_the_user_only`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/launch_hygiene.rs#L348) | Review F4 and audit A2: an app's private home and its own layer of the app folder, and the start's log, are for the user only, also with the usual umask (022) and when an older SAM made their folders readable for everyone. | 🆕 new |
| [`private_dirs_stops_below_its_base`](../crates/sam-core/src/util.rs#L816) | Review F4: `private_dirs` makes the folders from `dir` up to `base` private, those that exist already too, and never `base` or a folder above it: SAM's data folder and the person's own folders keep their modes. | 🆕 new |

**Code:** [`sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs), [`sam-core/src/host.rs`](../crates/sam-core/src/host.rs), [`sam-core/src/util.rs`](../crates/sam-core/src/util.rs)

<a id="11"></a>
### 11. Keys, passwords and the files of your other apps stay hidden, even with full home access.

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`a_grant_inside_sams_folder_does_not_show_it`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L6167) | SAM's folder is hidden also when a grant shows only something inside it (audit S-F13): the grant does not reach SAM's files. | ✅ |
| [`opened_files_are_judged_by_name_and_by_where_they_lead`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L6188) | Files named on the command line are judged with the home as named and as reached (a home under a link), by the name they are given (a file reached through `~/.config` is not shared), and a file a grant already shows is not added again. | ✅ |
| [`keys_start_places_and_sams_files_are_never_opened`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L6117) | Keys, credential stores, start places (also behind a link) and SAM's own files named on the command line are never shared, whatever the home is called; an ordinary file is, and so is a folder that only holds SAM's folder (SAM's folder is hidden inside it) (audit D-M2, S-F13). | ✅ |
| [`start_places_and_credential_stores_count_as_sensitive`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1304) | Every start place and credential store the sandbox locks or hides counts as sensitive, under any home; a drive under /run/media does not, though /run does (audit D-M2, D-M3). | ✅ |
| [`grants_of_parts_of_the_home_never_reach_start_places_or_secrets`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/home_grants.rs#L69) | Grants of parts of the home never reach start places or secrets. | ✅ |
| [`sams_own_folders_stay_hidden_inside_an_opened_folder`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L7051) | SAM's own folders stay hidden inside a folder opened with the app, and inside a runner's bind, at the path the bind shows them under. | ✅ |
| [`key_and_system_folders_count_as_sensitive`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1291) | Paths that hold keys or the whole system (~/.ssh, /etc, /) are recognized as sensitive; ordinary folders are not. | ✅ |
| [`every_breakout_attempt_from_a_real_sandbox_fails`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/breakout.rs#L156) | Every security promise holds inside a real sandbox: each breakout attempt fails. | ✅ |
