| [`every_hostile_appimage_fails_to_escape`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/evil.rs#L159) | Every hostile AppImage, added and started like any other, fails to get out of its sandbox: it cannot read keys, SAM's settings or another app's files; cannot write autostart entries, shell startup files or host temporary files; cannot type into the terminal, reach the network when it has none, see host processes or change its own app folder; cannot reach systemd, the Flatpak spawn portal, the keyring or a launcher service, nor take over a portal's name on the desktop bus; and a symlink or setuid bit in its image gains it nothing. | ✅ |
| [`credential_files_and_folders_in_the_home_are_masked`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L8605) | With the whole home granted, credential files are replaced by an empty file and credential folders by an empty folder. | ✅ |
| [`home_protection_is_prepared_only_for_a_writable_home`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L8632) | Protected folders are created in the real home only before a start that can write the home, never otherwise. | ✅ |
| [`sams_own_folders_stay_hidden_inside_a_granted_folder`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L6045) | SAM's own folders stay hidden when a granted folder contains them, and nothing extra is added when no grant shows them. | ✅ |
| [`user_folder_locations_stay_read_only_with_a_writable_home`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L7085) | With the home writable, the file that says where the user folders are stays read-only, so an app cannot point other apps' Documents or Downloads grant at another folder. | ✅ |
| [`a_linked_key_folder_is_hidden_also_when_only_its_target_is_granted`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5395) | A key folder kept behind a link is hidden where it points also when only the folder it points into is granted, not the home (security review 2026-11-23, SB-2: `~/.gnupg` a link into a granted `~/Sync` showed the keys, writable). | 🆕 new |
| [`places_behind_links_stay_protected_and_the_app_still_starts`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/linked_places.rs#L65) | Places behind links stay protected and the app still starts. | 🆕 new |
| [`places_named_by_the_homes_real_path_are_protected_where_the_app_sees_them`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5631) | With the home kept behind a link (`/home/u` a link to `/data/u`), a link in it that names the home's real path (`~/.config` to `/data/u/dots/config`, as tools that resolve the folder they run in write it) leads nowhere inside, where only `/home/u` is shown; yet the app reaches the place it points to under the home's name (`/home/u/dots/config`). | 🆕 new |
| [`sams_own_folders_behind_a_link_are_hidden_where_the_app_reaches_them`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5664) | SAM's own folders kept behind a link (`~/.config` a link, SAM's settings in `~/.config/sam`) are hidden where the app reaches them, never through the link: with the link leading to a place nothing grants, the empty folder on `~/.config/sam` stopped every start with the whole home writable (Bubblewrap could not make the folders above it); with the link leading into the home, the folder is hidden where it points (security review 2026-11-23, SB-6). | 🆕 new |
| [`folders_named_like_sams_are_hidden_while_sam_keeps_its_own_elsewhere`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L6590) | The folders where SAM keeps its own unless told otherwise (`~/.config/sam`, `~/.local/share/sam`) are among SAM's folders (`Paths::sam_dirs`) also while this SAM keeps its own elsewhere (`SAM_DATA_DIR=~/Games/sam`): a grant of something inside one is hidden with or without the whole home, and a terminal app's start line leaves it out, as the sandbox check expects. | 🆕 new |
| [`sams_folders_under_other_config_folders_are_hidden_not_only_read_only`](../crates/sam-sandbox/tests/sams_folders_under_other_config_folders.rs#L66) | With the whole home writable, the sandbox puts an empty folder in place of `~/cfg/sam` and `~/dat/sam` by name, also when its list of SAM's folders is empty, and a writable grant inside one is not bound: the sandbox check expects that grant hidden. | 🆕 new |
| [`sams_folders_hold_its_own_and_the_folders_named_like_them`](../crates/sam-core/src/paths.rs#L492) | SAM's own folders are its settings, data and unpack folders where they are kept, and the folders it keeps them in unless told otherwise (`~/.config/sam`, `~/.local/share/sam`), also while it keeps its own elsewhere (`SAM_DATA_DIR=~/Games/sam`), which the sandbox hides by name; each once (independent re-review of decision 9). | 🆕 new |
| [`sams_folders_by_name_follow_other_config_and_data_folders`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L1879) | SAM's folders by name are every protected place named `sam`: the usual two, and the same under `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and `XDG_DATA_HOME` (`~/cfg/sam`), which the sandbox now hides as SAM's own instead of only locking them read-only; a name that only begins like it, or another place in those folders, is none (independent re-review of decision 9). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_folder_named_like_sams_is_hidden_unless_that_would_cover_too_much`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L1966) | A place named like SAM's folders counts as SAM's, hidden wherever the app reaches it with links followed, unless hiding it would cover too much (independent reviews of decision 9): - a real folder counts, also with a grant inside it; - a link counts wherever it leads, inside or outside the home, also through a link above it (`~/dat` leading out of the home): another SAM may keep its settings in `~/Sync/sam` with `~/.local/share/sam` a link there, and the whole home or a grant of `~/Sync` showed them while every link counted as an ordinary start place; - a link to the home or a folder above it never counts, nor does a real folder that is the home where it really is; - a link counts neither when the start shows only folders inside the place it leads to (`~/.config/sam` planted as a link to `~/work`, `~/work/proj` granted), but it does when the start shows that place whole (exactly `~/work`, or the whole home), which SAM cannot tell from another SAM's folder kept there: it stays hidden as before; - where this SAM keeps its own counts wherever it leads. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_link_named_like_sams_folder_never_hides_the_home`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L6686) | A folder named like SAM's that is a link (`~/.config/sam` planted as a link to the home by an app that could write `~/.config`) never makes the home count as SAM's folder: no start covers the home with an empty folder, a grant below it stays shown and a writable one is shown read-only as below any start place, not left out. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_protected_place_that_really_is_the_home_is_named`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L2104) | A protected place that really is the home (`~/.config/sam` planted as a link to it, or a start place such as `~/.config/fish` leading there) and that the start does not hide as SAM's is named: the sandbox's lock on it lands on the whole home, which the app then sees read-only, and its note, its check and the app page take this answer (independent review of decision 9: the check expected the home writable, and a grant in the home was said to be "inside ~/.config/sam"). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_link_named_like_sams_folder_covers_no_grant_it_only_holds`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L6833) | `~/.config/sam` planted as a link to `~/work` by an app that could write `~/.config`, while SAM keeps its own elsewhere, never covers a grant inside `~/work` with an empty folder: a writable `~/work/proj` is shown read-only, as inside any start place, and the start line names it. | 🆕 new |
| [`another_sams_folders_behind_a_link_stay_hidden`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L6786) | Another SAM's folders kept elsewhere through a link (`~/.local/share/sam` a link to `~/Sync/sam`, or `~/.config` a link out of the home) stay hidden where the app reaches them while this SAM keeps its own in `~/Games/sam`: with the whole home, with a grant of the folder above (`~/Sync`) and with a grant of exactly that folder, an empty folder comes after every bind that shows it, and the start line does not name a grant it covers. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_home_protections_hold_whatever_else_is_granted`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/home_guards.rs#L88) | The home protections hold whatever else is granted. | ✅ |
| [`a_folder_a_switch_names_is_hidden_but_never_used_in_a_release_build`](../crates/sam-core/src/paths.rs#L534) | A folder that `SAM_DATA_DIR` or `SAM_CONFIG_DIR` names stays one of SAM's folders that every sandbox hides, also in a release build, which keeps its settings and data in the standard folders and never uses that folder: an installed SAM that once kept its data in `~/Games/sam` left its app records and the other apps' private homes there, and with `~/Games` granted an app read them (batch review of the change that made release builds ignore these switches). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_folder_a_switch_names_is_hidden_unless_that_would_cover_too_much`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L2221) | A folder that one of SAM's folder switches names (`SAM_DATA_DIR`, `SAM_CONFIG_DIR`) counts as SAM's by the rules for the folders named like SAM's, whatever its own name, so hiding it never covers too much (batch review of the change that made release builds ignore these switches): - a real folder counts, inside or outside the home, also with a grant inside it or of the folder above it; - the home, a folder above it (`/` too) and a way there (`~/work/..`) never count, nor does a link to the home: nothing then covers the home, and SAM's folders by name stay as they are; - a link counts neither where the start shows only folders inside the place it leads to (`~/work/proj` granted), but does where the start shows that place whole; - one that is where this SAM keeps its own is listed once, its own first, the folders by name next and the switches' folders last. | 🆕 new |
| [`an_old_switch_folder_stays_hidden_from_a_real_sandbox`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/switch_folders.rs#L68) | An old switch folder stays hidden from a real sandbox. | 🆕 new |
| [`an_extra_folder_that_leads_to_the_whole_computer_asks_first`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1364) | An extra folder written so that it mounts the whole computer, the whole home or a place found through `..` is Dangerous, warned about and asks first (review F1: `/.` read Harmless and a preset applied it without a question). | 🆕 new |
| [`no_later_bind_shows_a_hidden_credential_again`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9425) | A hidden credential is never shown again by a bind after it: with `~/.git-credentials` a link into `~/.config/git` (a protected place, bound read-only onto itself), the protected place came after the hiding and showed the real file (mutation run 2026-11-23). | 🆕 new |
| [`credential_places_of_any_kind_are_hidden`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9476) | A credential place that is neither a file nor a folder is hidden too: `~/.netrc` as a named pipe a password manager feeds (so the secret never lies on disk) or a socket was left showing, and the app could read the secret from it. | 🆕 new |
| [`credentials_behind_links_pipes_and_sockets_stay_hidden`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/linked_credentials.rs#L54) | Credentials behind links pipes and sockets stay hidden. | 🆕 new |
| [`git_credentials_under_config_git_stay_hidden`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/git_credentials.rs#L64) | Git credentials under config git stay hidden. | 🆕 new |
| [`credential_stores_follow_other_config_and_data_folders`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L1905) | The credential stores follow `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and `XDG_DATA_HOME` inside the home as the start places do: with `~/cfg`, Git's passwords are kept in `~/cfg/git/credentials`, which was only locked read-only, so an app with the whole home could read them. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_font_folder_link_never_leads_out_of_what_the_app_is_shown`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/planted_bind_links.rs#L401) | Each rule on its own, for `~/.fonts`: - with the whole home writable, a link to a folder outside the home that the app is not shown (the app may have made it) is not followed; - in a private home, a link to a socket folder in `/tmp` (another program made it; the app cannot change the home) is not followed; - in a private home, a link to your SSH keys is not followed. | 🆕 new |
| [`an_own_place_never_leads_to_the_home_keys_or_sams_folders`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L2992) | A place SAM shows on its own (`~/.fonts`) never leads to the home, a folder above it, a key folder (also where it is kept behind a link), a credential store, a folder holding a protected place (`~/.config`) or SAM's own folders, whoever made the link: in a private home nothing else hides what such a bind shows. | 🆕 new |
| [`sams_folders_are_guarded_where_they_really_lead`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L3128) | SAM's own folders are guarded also where they really lead, not only as configured: with the unpack folder set to `~/Apps/sam` and `~/Apps` a link to another disk, `~/.fonts` made a link to the real place of that folder (in an earlier start with the whole home writable) was bound in a private home and showed every unpacked app; and with the home kept behind a link (`/home/u` a link to `/data/u`) and SAM's data folder set to `~/Games/sam`, `~/.fonts` made a link to `/data/u/Games/sam` showed the other apps' private homes. | 🆕 new |
| [`with_the_whole_home_writable_the_font_places_stay_bound_read_only`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L3223) | With the whole home writable, the font places stay bound read-only although a guarded place leads to the home: with hidden files on, the app made `~/.aws` (or `~/.ssh`, missing while the keys are granted) a link to the home in one start, so in the next every font place led into a guarded place and was left out, and the home showed the fonts and the fonts settings writable to the app. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_guarded_link_to_the_home_keeps_the_font_places_read_only`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L8173) | With the whole home writable and hidden files on, the app made `~/.aws` a link to the home in one start: in the next, every font place led into a guarded place, SAM left out their read-only binds, and the home showed `~/.fonts`, `~/.local/share/fonts` and `~/.config/fontconfig` writable, so the app could plant fonts and fontconfig rules that programs outside the sandbox load. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_font_place_another_grant_shows_writable_stays_bound_read_only`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L3304) | Where another grant of this start shows a font place writable (an extra writable folder `~/.config` in a private home), the place stays bound read-only although it leads into a guarded place: with hidden files on, the app made `~/.config/vivaldi` a link to `~/.config/fontconfig` in one start, so in the next the fonts settings were left out and the extra folder showed them writable. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_writable_extra_folder_keeps_the_font_and_theme_places_in_it_read_only`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L8366) | In a private or read-only home with hidden files on, a font or theme place inside an extra writable folder stays bound read-only after it, although a sensitive place leads to it: the app made `~/.config/vivaldi` a link to `~/.config/fontconfig` (or `~/.local/share/keyrings` one to `~/.local/share/fonts`, or `~/.config/vivaldi` one to `~/.config/gtk-3.0`) in one start, so in the next SAM left the read-only bind out as leading into a guarded place, and the writable folder showed the fonts settings, the fonts or the theme files writable: rules that programs outside the sandbox load ([`own_bind`]). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_font_link_to_the_real_place_of_a_home_behind_a_link_binds_no_guarded_place`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L8217) | With the home kept behind a link (`/home/u` a link to `/data/u`) and writable, the app made a missing `~/.fonts` a link to the real place of `~/.ssh` (`/data/u/.ssh`, which it can read in `/proc/self/mountinfo`): that place lies in the home where it really is, so the whole home counted as showing it writable, and the next start bound `/data/u/.ssh` read-only at that name. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_font_link_to_the_real_place_of_a_home_behind_a_link_shows_no_keys`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/planted_bind_links.rs#L898) | With the home kept behind a link (`~` a link to its real folder, as `/home` to `/var/home` on some systems) and the whole home writable, the app makes a missing `~/.fonts` a link to the real place of `~/.ssh` (or of SAM's own folder `~/.local/share/sam`) in one start. | 🆕 new |

**Code:** [`sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs), [`sam-core/src/risk.rs`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs), [`sam-core/src/paths.rs`](../crates/sam-core/src/paths.rs), [`sam-core/src/places.rs`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs)

<a id="12"></a>
### 12. Network: none, "This computer only" (selected ports), or full.

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`localhost_network_leaves_netns_to_pasta`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4706) | With "this computer only", the network namespace is left to pasta. | ✅ |
| [`only_listed_host_ports`](../crates/sam-runner/src/netns.rs#L184) | With "this computer only", the app reaches only the ports on this computer the user lists. | ✅ |
| [`the_apps_ports_listen_on_loopback_only`](../crates/sam-runner/src/netns.rs#L204) | The app's ports are offered on this computer's loopback addresses only, for IPv4 and IPv6 alike, never on every address (audit L-F7). | ✅ |
| [`well_known_local_services_are_never_taken_over`](../crates/sam-runner/src/netns.rs#L237) | Local services whose clients would hand an app control or secrets are never taken over automatically. | ✅ |
| [`the_apps_ports_are_offered_on_this_computer_only`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/local_network.rs#L106) | The apps ports are offered on this computer only. | ✅ |
| [`each_permission_grants_exactly_its_access_in_a_real_sandbox`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/matrix.rs#L169) | Inside a real sandbox, each permission grants exactly its access and nothing more. | ✅ |
| [`a_pasta_command_ending_in_the_separator_is_refused_not_a_panic`](../crates/sam-runner/src/launch.rs#L2597) | A pasta command ending in the separator is refused not a panic. | ✅ |
| [`the_status_shell_goes_between_the_separator_and_the_program`](../crates/sam-runner/src/launch.rs#L2604) | The status shell goes between the separator and the program. | ✅ |
| [`name_server_settings_are_shown_with_the_network_only`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9032) | The host's name server settings are shown with the network only: an app without it sees none of them. | 🆕 new |
| [`only_the_ports_the_user_listed_are_forwarded`](../crates/sam-core/src/permissions.rs#L1561) | "This computer only" forwards exactly the ports the user listed: each once, sorted, without port 0, and never DNS (port 53) from the host. | 🆕 new |

**Code:** [`sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs), [`sam-runner/src/netns.rs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/netns.rs)

<a id="13"></a>
### 13. The app reaches only allowed desktop services (a filtered D-Bus) and can't start programs outside its sandbox.

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`the_sockets_follow_the_sessions_variables`](../crates/sam-core/src/host.rs#L962) | The sockets given to a sandbox follow the user's session: a set variable names the socket (WAYLAND_DISPLAY=wayland-7 is not wayland-0, which may be another compositor's), and an empty one counts as unset, so the usual places are used. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_release_build_keeps_no_dbus_log`](../crates/sam-runner/src/dbus_proxy.rs#L328) | A release build keeps no D-Bus log, whatever `SAM_DBUS_LOG` says; a developer build writes it to the file named there (owner decision 16 of 2026-11-23). | 🆕 new |
| [`the_user_name_is_users_or_the_home_folders`](../crates/sam-core/src/host.rs#L1268) | The sandbox's user name is `$USER`; where that is empty or unset, the home folder's name, and "user" where there is none. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_32_bit_start_program_keeps_the_syscall_filter_on`](../crates/sam-profile/src/lib.rs#L1057) | The syscall filter stays on for every app: a 32-bit start program switched it off, so an app could choose to run without it (review K-3; the filter now covers 32-bit calls). | 🆕 new |
| [`without_nested_sandboxes_clone_cannot_make_a_user_namespace`](../crates/sam-runner/src/kernel.rs#L1252) | Review K-2: without nested sandboxes the filter refused unshare() but let clone() and clone3() make a new user namespace, which the unpack sandbox did not stop otherwise. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_32_bit_app_gets_its_calls_filtered_not_switched_off`](../crates/sam-runner/src/kernel.rs#L1315) | A 32 bit app gets its calls filtered not switched off. | 🆕 new |
| [`every_blocked_call_is_blocked_for_32_bit_apps_too`](../crates/sam-runner/src/kernel.rs#L1499) | Every row of the filter has its 32-bit x86 numbers (kexec_file_load has none): a call added to the filter is not forgotten for 32-bit apps. | 🆕 new |
| [`each_call_number_is_in_one_row`](../crates/sam-runner/src/kernel.rs#L1516) | No call is named twice and no call number is in two rows, neither this processor's nor a 32-bit one, so one row says everything a call gets. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_32_bit_start_program_gets_the_filters_32_bit_part`](../crates/sam-runner/src/launch.rs#L2873) | A 32-bit start program gets the filter's 32-bit part (review K-3); a script among 32-bit files, or an app nobody analyzed, does not. | 🆕 new |
| [`links_to_other_programs_ask_first`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L6070) | Links leave the sandbox through the portal: web and mail links go to the person's browser or mail program as asked; any other kind (`steam://`, `vscode://`, a program's own scheme) makes the portal ask first with its app chooser, so an app cannot start another program by a link (audit L-F14). | ✅ |
| [`the_link_portal_check_finds_the_ask_option`](../crates/sam-runner/src/contracts.rs#L774) | The link portal's ask option is found in its description, and its absence is reported (stand-in descriptions; the real one is checked by `sam-jail doctor --contracts` and on CI). | ✅ |
| [`function_and_signal_rules_count_in_the_risk_word`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L903) | Single functions and signals of a service count in the risk word: a function of a service that can act outside the sandbox is Dangerous, any other function Think twice, a signal Harmless (audit D-H2: they counted for nothing). | ✅ |
| [`a_change_that_adds_a_service_function_asks_first`](../crates/sam-profile/src/lib.rs#L1124) | A preset (or any change) that adds a function of a service asks first, as one that adds the X11 display does (audit D-H2: a preset with "may call systemd" applied without a question). | ✅ |
| [`bus_names_follow_the_rules_of_the_bus`](../crates/sam-core/src/permissions.rs#L1404) | An app may not own a bus name of a desktop service or of a service that starts on demand; its own application names stay allowed. | ✅ |
| [`media_controls_talk_but_ask_for_no_wildcard_name`](../crates/sam-core/src/permissions.rs#L1427) | Media controls let the app talk to the screen saver and power services; they no longer ask for the media-player wildcard, which was always refused (audit L-Q1): the app's own player name is learned instead. | ✅ |
| [`wanted_names_are_kept_once_and_at_most_eight`](../crates/sam-runner/src/dbus_proxy.rs#L489) | A name is kept once, and no more than eight in all. | ✅ |
| [`a_record_line_that_is_not_text_does_not_end_the_reading`](../crates/sam-runner/src/dbus_proxy.rs#L476) | B2 R83: a line of the proxy's record that is not valid UTF-8 (the proxy prints parts of the app's messages unchecked) does not end the reading: the lines after it are still read, so the proxy never writes into a closed pipe, and a name asked for later is learned. | 🆕 new |
| [`an_app_gets_the_name_it_asks_for_only_when_nobody_uses_it`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/own_names.rs#L46) | An app gets the name it asks for only when nobody uses it. | ✅ |
| [`a_name_is_allowed_only_when_nobody_else_has_it`](../crates/sam-runner/src/own_names.rs#L190) | A name the app asks for is refused when it belongs to an installed app (audit D-M6: a terminal's name runs host commands), to a desktop protocol, to an on-demand service (also through a wildcard, L-F13), or to another app in SAM; its own name passes; a media player's name passes with the media switch (L-Q1). | ✅ |
| [`menu_ids_are_the_file_names`](../crates/sam-runner/src/own_names.rs#L232) | Menu entry ids come from file names, subfolders joined with `-`; only `.desktop` files count. | ✅ |
| [`the_proxy_record_names_what_the_app_wanted_to_own`](../crates/sam-runner/src/dbus_proxy.rs#L456) | Only the proxy's "wants to own" lines name a name to learn, and only a well-formed one (what the app printed never reaches this). | ✅ |
| [`the_apps_own_words_never_give_it_a_bus_name`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs#L1004) | What the app prints never gets it a bus name, whatever name it claims it needs (a terminal's name runs host commands; audit D-M6): names come only from the D-Bus filter's record (`own_names`). | ✅ |
| [`the_filter_refuses_x32_odd_sockets_and_the_siblings`](../crates/sam-runner/src/kernel.rs#L930) | The filter, applied by the real kernel in a child process: every call through the x32 ABI is refused (audit S-F9; x32 `ptrace` has its own number), the siblings of blocked calls are blocked too (S-F10), and a socket opens only as local, internet or netlink, Bluetooth only with its switch (S-F7). | ✅ |
| [`the_filter_checks_the_architecture_seccompiler_checks`](../crates/sam-runner/src/kernel.rs#L898) | Review R71: the architecture check that opens [`seccomp_program`] (for an app that is not a 32-bit app) is the one seccompiler builds for this processor (same field, same number, same end), for the processor the test runs on. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_strict_filter_blocks_every_call_its_text_names`](../crates/sam-runner/src/kernel.rs#L1002) | Review R68: the strict filter promised to block access to other processes and the NUMA memory calls, but let through `process_madvise`, `pidfd_getfd` (another process's descriptors, the same right as `ptrace`), `kcmp` and three NUMA calls. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_baseline_blocks_what_no_desktop_app_needs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/kernel.rs#L1043) | Review R69: the baseline blocks what Flatpak's default filter blocks and no desktop app needs: `chroot` and the NUMA memory calls. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_socket_pair_opens_only_the_listed_families_too`](../crates/sam-runner/src/kernel.rs#L1085) | Review R66: `socketpair` makes its sockets as `socket` does, but only `socket` had the family limit, so a pair of crypto, packet or virtual-machine sockets reached the kernel (and loaded its module; TIPC even works as a pair). | 🆕 new |
| [`the_filter_reads_arguments_as_wide_as_the_kernel_does`](../crates/sam-runner/src/kernel.rs#L1133) | Review R74: the kernel reads `ioctl`'s request and `socket`'s family as 32-bit numbers and drops the upper half of the register. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_filter_reaches_bubblewrap_as_whole_instructions`](../crates/sam-runner/src/kernel.rs#L1189) | Bubblewrap reads the filter as eight bytes per instruction. | ✅ |
| [`the_filter_decides_every_call_as_recorded`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/seccomp_decisions.rs#L273) | The filter built on this processor answers as recorded for it, under all 16 settings: for call numbers 0 to 1023 of x86_64, i386, aarch64 and 32-bit ARM, the same numbers with the x32 bit set and four extreme numbers, each argument the filter reads varied alone over a fixed list of values. | 🆕 new |
| [`an_app_cannot_open_the_kernels_rarely_used_doors`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/kernel_surface.rs#L132) | An app cannot open the kernels rarely used doors. | ✅ |
| [`own_names_that_are_refused`](../crates/sam-core/src/permissions.rs#L1448) | Own names that are refused. | ✅ |
| [`wildcards_and_versions_of_dangerous_services_are_dangerous`](../crates/sam-core/src/permissions.rs#L1586) | Wildcards and versioned names of dangerous desktop services count as dangerous (warned about, rated Dangerous, never owned), and so does any wildcard over the portal's names; ordinary names do not. | 🆕 new |
| [`switched_on_integrations_reach_the_bus_filter_once`](../crates/sam-core/src/permissions.rs#L1699) | A switched-on integration reaches the enforced bus filter: every one of its names is allowed, next to the user's own entries, and each name only once. | 🆕 new |
| [`always_blocked_names_stay_blocked_everywhere`](../crates/sam-core/src/permissions.rs#L1625) | The always-blocked names stay blocked whatever else allows them (the setting's promise): an exact entry and a wildcard entry are left out of talking, owning, calls and signals alike, also when a wildcard elsewhere could reach them or an integration asks for them; names nobody blocked stay. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_filter_never_names_an_always_blocked_name`](../crates/sam-runner/src/dbus_proxy.rs#L347) | The proxy's filter never names an always-blocked name, in any of its rules, also when the rules it is given still hold one: the second line behind `effective_session_bus`. | 🆕 new |
| [`an_always_blocked_name_cannot_be_owned_from_inside_the_sandbox`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/always_blocked_names.rs#L33) | An always blocked name cannot be owned from inside the sandbox. | 🆕 new |
| [`known_desktop_services_reach_only_their_own_objects`](../crates/sam-runner/src/dbus_proxy.rs#L375) | B2 R76: a desktop service SAM allows by default or through a switch gets calls and signals on its own objects only, never a plain `--talk`, which would open every object of the program behind it (on Plasma 6, plasmashell's clipboard history and script runner behind the notification service). | 🆕 new |
| [`default_desktop_services_reach_only_their_own_objects`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/default_bus_names.rs#L57) | Default desktop services reach only their own objects. | 🆕 new |
| [`blocked_names_win_over_wildcards`](../crates/sam-core/src/permissions.rs#L1726) | A bus name the user blocks stays blocked even when a wildcard in the allowed list would match it. | ✅ |
| [`only_real_socket_paths_are_taken_from_a_dbus_address`](../crates/sam-core/src/host.rs#L1336) | Only real socket paths are taken from a D-Bus address; abstract sockets are not. | ✅ |
| [`own_names_from_the_id_and_the_window_class`](../crates/sam-profile/src/lib.rs#L1221) | The bus names an app may own come from its application ID and window class, never from protocol or desktop-service names. | ✅ |
| [`filtered_dbus_uses_proxy`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4320) | The filtered desktop bus reaches the app only through SAM's proxy. | ✅ |
| [`the_app_runs_as_the_user_and_host_tools_are_replaced_by_stand_ins`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4558) | The app runs as the user and host tools are replaced by stand ins. | ✅ |
| [`raw_arguments_missing_a_value_stop_the_start`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4605) | Extra Bubblewrap arguments that end with an option missing its values stop the start: the option would take SAM's `--remount-ro /` after it, and the root would stay writable (review B2 R58). | 🆕 new |
| [`the_systemd_run_stand_in_skips_options_with_a_value`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4661) | The systemd-run stand-in skips every option of systemd-run that takes its value as the next argument, so that value never runs as the command (review B2 R61: `--job-mode fail` ran `fail`). | 🆕 new |
| [`without_nested_namespaces_user_namespaces_are_disabled`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4739) | Without nested namespaces, the sandbox disables user namespaces and starts through SAM's helper. | ✅ |
| [`extra_sockets_must_be_sockets`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4906) | Extra sockets are passed in only when they really are sockets. | ✅ |
| [`tray_names_follow_watcher_permission`](../crates/sam-runner/src/dbus_proxy.rs#L504) | An app may register a tray icon only while the tray service is allowed. | ✅ |
| [`the_syscall_filter_compiles_and_blocks_mount_and_io_uring`](../crates/sam-runner/src/kernel.rs#L1200) | The syscall filter compiles in every variant and always blocks mount and io_uring. | ✅ |
| [`every_breakout_attempt_from_a_real_sandbox_fails`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/breakout.rs#L156) | Every security promise holds inside a real sandbox: each breakout attempt fails. | ✅ |
| [`every_hostile_appimage_fails_to_escape`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/evil.rs#L159) | Every hostile AppImage, added and started like any other, fails to get out of its sandbox: it cannot read keys, SAM's settings or another app's files; cannot write autostart entries, shell startup files or host temporary files; cannot type into the terminal, reach the network when it has none, see host processes or change its own app folder; cannot reach systemd, the Flatpak spawn portal, the keyring or a launcher service, nor take over a portal's name on the desktop bus; and a symlink or setuid bit in its image gains it nothing. | ✅ |
| [`a_socket_counts_as_ready_only_when_it_accepts`](../crates/sam-runner/src/dbus_proxy.rs#L598) | The proxy counts as ready only when its socket accepts connections, not when the file merely exists (regression: the sandbox check sometimes found the session bus refused right after the start). | ✅ |
| [`only_a_real_socket_counts_as_a_desktop_service`](../crates/sam-core/src/host.rs#L1060) | Only a real socket counts as a desktop service. | ✅ |
| [`services_that_start_on_demand_are_recognized`](../crates/sam-core/src/host.rs#L1032) | Services that start on demand are recognized from their folders, including the user's own (contract dbus-activatable). | ✅ |
| [`stand_ins_cover_the_real_tool_also_behind_a_link`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L3818) | The stand-in for a tool that would start programs outside the sandbox covers the real file, also where the tool is a link (audit S-F14); a tool that is not installed needs none. | ✅ |
| [`an_extra_socket_swapped_for_a_link_by_the_app_is_not_passed_in`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4935) | An extra socket the app could have swapped for a link (it sits in a folder the app may write) is not passed in when it is a link: the bind followed the link to whatever socket it named, such as the real session bus (review SB-4). | 🆕 new |
| [`an_extra_socket_in_a_folder_the_app_can_change_is_not_passed_in`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4997) | An extra socket below a folder the app can change is not passed in, with a note; in a folder the app may only read, it is. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_way_through_forty_links_is_followed_and_one_through_more_is_not`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5588) | Both walks follow links as Linux does: a way through 40 links ends where they lead, one through 41 counts as a loop (reached nowhere, and possibly made by the app), and the check of a listed socket ends in time on a loop (the walk to a place has its loop test above). | 🆕 new |
| [`an_extra_socket_that_leads_to_a_dangerous_one_is_not_passed_in`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5078) | An extra socket whose link leads to a socket SAM calls dangerous (here a D-Bus socket) is not passed in: what the app gets is rated, not the name it was listed by (review SB-4). | 🆕 new |
| [`an_app_cannot_turn_its_extra_socket_into_a_link_to_another_one`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/socket_links.rs#L68) | An app cannot turn its extra socket into a link to another one. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_font_folder_link_the_app_planted_never_reaches_host_sockets`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/planted_bind_links.rs#L309) | `~/.fonts`, planted as a link to a socket folder in `/tmp`. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_fontconfig_link_the_app_planted_never_reaches_host_sockets`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/planted_bind_links.rs#L319) | `~/.config/fontconfig`, planted as a link to a socket folder in `/tmp`. | 🆕 new |
| [`an_extra_folder_link_the_app_planted_never_reaches_host_sockets`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/planted_bind_links.rs#L338) | An extra read-only folder that did not exist yet, planted as a link to a socket folder in `/tmp`; also one planted as a link to a folder outside the home and outside `/tmp` that the app is not shown (not a place of sockets: the link rule alone, and the start names it). | 🆕 new |
| [`fonts_themes_and_extra_folders_never_follow_a_planted_link`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9500) | The places SAM shows on its own (`~/.fonts`, `~/.local/share/fonts`, `~/.config/fontconfig`, the theme files) and the extra folders never follow a link the app could have planted out of what it is shown, nor a link to where programs outside the sandbox listen: with the whole home writable and `~/.fonts` a link to `/tmp/tmux-…`, the start bound the host's folder over its own `/tmp`. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_user_folder_link_the_app_planted_never_reaches_host_sockets`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/planted_bind_links.rs#L498) | A user folder SAM binds on its own: in a private home with `~/Sync` writable and Documents set to `~/Sync/Documents` (not made yet), the app makes it a link to a stand-in for tmux's socket folder in one start; in the next start SAM leaves Documents out, and the app reaches the socket neither where the link leads nor through the link. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_link_the_app_planted_never_makes_a_read_only_home_writable`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/planted_bind_links.rs#L519) | With the home read-only and Downloads writable, the app makes an extra writable folder in Downloads, not made yet, a link to the home in one start; in the next start it can write neither in the home nor through the link: the read-only home stays read-only. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_link_planted_in_a_user_folder_of_a_writable_home_is_not_followed`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/planted_bind_links.rs#L578) | With the whole home writable and Downloads set read-only, the app cannot plant anything in Downloads: SAM mounts it read-only at its own access over the writable home. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_planted_link_never_makes_a_read_only_home_writable`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9661) | With the home read-only and Downloads writable, an extra writable folder in Downloads not made yet is not bound; the app makes it a link to the home, and the next start bound the whole home writable over its read-only bind. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_user_folder_never_follows_a_planted_link`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9736) | A user folder bound on its own never follows a link the app could have planted, nor one to where programs outside the sandbox listen: with `~/Sync` writable and Documents set to `~/Sync/Documents`, not made yet, the app made it a link to `/tmp/tmux-…`, and the next start bound the host's sockets over its own `/tmp`. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_user_folder_in_a_writable_home_counts_as_one_the_app_can_change`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L3344) | With the whole home writable, a user folder set read-only is shown through the home, writable: a link the app made in it is not followed out of what the home and the user folders show, for a read-only bind or a writable one. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_writable_bind_follows_a_planted_link_only_into_what_is_shown_writable`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L3390) | A writable bind follows a link the app could have planted only into what the home and the user folders show writable: with the home read-only, a writable extra folder in a writable user folder that the app made a link to the home, or to a read-only user folder, is left out (bound there, it made the read-only home writable). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_writable_bind_never_follows_a_planted_link_into_a_read_only_folder`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L3469) | A writable bind follows a link the app could have planted only into what the sandbox really shows writable, each folder at the access it is mounted at. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_planted_link_never_makes_a_read_only_user_folder_writable`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/planted_bind_links.rs#L643) | With the whole home writable and Documents set read-only, SAM mounts Documents read-only over the home. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_user_folder_behind_a_planted_link_is_left_out`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L3571) | A user folder SAM binds on its own is left out where a link on its way lies in a folder the app can change and leads out of what the home shows, or where it leads to programs listening outside the sandbox, whoever made the link (with `~/Sync` writable, `~/Sync/Documents` made a link to `/tmp/tmux-…` showed the host's tmux sockets): where both hold, for the listening programs. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_linked_place_is_left_out_where_a_planted_link_leads_out_or_to_host_services`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L2468) | One rule for every bind through links (`~/.fonts`, an extra folder): with the whole home writable, a link in it is not followed out of what the home shows (the app may have planted it), also when its target does not exist yet, and for one to where programs outside the sandbox listen that is the reason given; one that stays in the home is followed. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_link_within_a_linked_home_is_followed_like_one_in_a_plain_home`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L2596) | A home kept behind a link (Fedora Silverblue's `/home` a link to `/var/home`, or `~` a link to `/data/u`) shows everything in its real place: with the whole home writable, `~/.fonts` a link to a folder in the real home is followed and bound, as in a plain home. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_left_out_note_never_says_the_place_is_hidden`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L2666) | A left-out note, for a planted link, a link to where programs outside the sandbox listen or a guarded place, never says the place is hidden, since other grants may still show it, and for a planted link the rule weighs only what the home and the user folders show (for a user folder the home alone). | 🆕 new |
| [`the_places_where_outside_programs_listen_are_known`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L2366) | The places where programs outside the sandbox listen: the whole computer; anything in `/tmp` and `/var/tmp` (a socket directly there, a socket folder by any name) and a folder holding one; the host's runtime folder and SAM's runtime folders wherever they are, and a folder holding one; `/run` but not drives under `/run/media`; `/proc` and `/dev`. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_way_to_a_path_follows_every_link_also_one_that_leads_nowhere_yet`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L2312) | The way to a path follows every link as Linux does: each named in the folder that really holds it, `..` after a link going back from where the link leads, a link whose target does not exist yet followed to that target (a bind a moment later would show it if it appears), and a loop stuck at its 41st link. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_read_only_grant_never_hides_a_link_the_app_planted`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/planted_bind_links.rs#L715) | A read-only grant never hides a link the app planted: with the whole home writable and `~/x` an extra read-only folder, the app makes `~/x` a link to `~/.local/share` and `~/.local/share/fonts` a link to a folder it is not shown, in one start. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_font_folder_link_never_reaches_sockets_by_any_name`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/planted_bind_links.rs#L771) | A link nobody in the sandbox could have planted (`~/.fonts` in a private home) never reaches a place where programs outside the sandbox listen, by any name: a socket directly in `/tmp` (as PostgreSQL keeps its own), a socket folder in `/tmp` by a name no list knows (`tmate-…`), and the host's runtime folder kept outside `/run` and `/tmp` (as `/mnt/wslg/runtime-dir`). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_font_folder_link_through_a_link_in_tmp_never_reaches_sockets`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/planted_bind_links.rs#L852) | Where the way ends is not all: a link in `/tmp` made outside the sandbox may send it on to a socket folder SAM does not know by its place (on WSLg the host's `/tmp/.X11-unix` is a link to `/mnt/wslg/.X11-unix`). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_link_in_tmp_that_sends_the_way_elsewhere_is_never_followed`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L2886) | Where the way ends is not all: a link in `/tmp` made outside the sandbox (on WSLg the host's `/tmp/.X11-unix`, a link to `/mnt/wslg/.X11-unix`) sends the way on to a place SAM does not know as one where programs listen. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_read_only_grant_never_makes_a_folder_one_the_app_cannot_change`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L3652) | A read-only grant never makes a folder one the app cannot change: with the whole home writable and `~/x` an extra read-only folder, the app made `~/x` a link to `~/.local/share` and `~/.local/share/fonts` a link to a folder it is not shown in one start, and the next start with the same permissions bound that folder as the fonts (counted read-only by `~/x`, `~/.local/share` hid the link). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_folder_this_start_shows_writable_counts_as_one_the_app_can_change`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L3707) | A folder this start shows writable besides the permissions (a folder opened with the app, writable; a writable bind of SAM's) holds links the app may have made, as a writable grant does: in a private home, an extra folder named by a link in such a folder is not followed out of what the app is shown. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_folder_this_start_shows_writable_holds_links_the_app_may_have_made`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9875) | A folder this start shows writable besides the permissions holds links the app may have made, as `write_grants` counts it: a folder opened with the app while opened files are writable, and a writable bind of SAM's. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_link_to_any_place_where_outside_programs_listen_is_left_out`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L3744) | A link nobody in the sandbox could have planted (`~/.fonts` in a private home) to where programs outside the sandbox listen is left out, and the start says why: to a socket directly in `/tmp`, to a socket folder in `/tmp` by a name no list knows (`tmate-…`), to the host's runtime folder kept in `/tmp` or anywhere else, and to SAM's runtime folders wherever they are. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_link_to_sams_runtime_folders_is_never_followed`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9925) | SAM's runtime folders hold the sockets of SAM's helpers for every running app (the D-Bus proxy, the sound relay): wherever they are, a link to them is not followed, whoever made it. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_runtime_base_at_the_top_of_the_computer_never_counts_as_sams_runtime_folder`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9964) | SAM's runtime folders are this start's folder and the two above it, never a folder at the top of the computer: with the runtime base there (`XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/` gives `/sam`, `SAM_RUNTIME_DIR=/srv`), counting `/usr` as one left out every extra folder that leads anywhere in it (`/usr/share`) as a place where programs outside the sandbox listen. | 🆕 new |
| [`an_extra_socket_is_passed_in_only_as_dangerous_as_its_name`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5107) | A link whose listed name is dangerous for another reason than where it leads is not passed in either: a name that looks like a key agent (`x-ssh-agent.sock`) leading to the session bus was rated as a key agent, not as the bus (review of owner decision 11 of 2026-11-23). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_docker_socket_may_lead_to_podman_but_not_to_the_bus`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5142) | Docker, Podman and containerd count as one kind: podman-docker's `docker.sock` linked to Podman's socket is passed in, while a `docker.sock` leading to the session bus is not (review of owner decision 11 of 2026-11-23). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_wildcard_over_the_portal_is_dangerous`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1494) | A wildcard that covers the portal's own name lets every portal call through, past SAM's choice of interfaces: Dangerous. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_terminal_that_runs_commands_is_dangerous_to_reach`](../crates/sam-core/src/risk.rs#L1514) | A terminal that runs any command it is sent is Dangerous to reach, by name, a wildcard over it or a function rule, so adding it asks first (audit D-M6: `set APP dbus.session.allow org.gnome.Terminal` added no risk and asked nothing). | 🆕 new |
| [`notes_about_a_missing_bus_come_only_when_it_is_missing`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9297) | The notes that a device or input method needs a bus come only when it is asked for and the bus is not granted. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_missing_dbus_proxy_is_named_only_where_the_filter_needs_it`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L648) | A filtered session bus on a computer without xdg-dbus-proxy (where SAM then gives the app no session bus) says so; with the proxy installed, or without a filtered bus, it does not. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_status_names_a_missing_dbus_proxy`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4543) | Bubblewrap's status says when xdg-dbus-proxy is missing (the filtered session bus is then off), and only then. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_app_gets_the_users_own_user_and_group_ids`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4499) | The app's user and group are the person's own, each where it belongs: on bwrap's command line and in the passwd and group files the sandbox shows (the group is never the user's number, nor root's). | 🆕 new |
| [`added_instance_names_and_tray_look_alikes_are_checked`](../crates/sam-runner/src/dbus_proxy.rs#L519) | Audit L-F13: the numbered instance names SAM adds to an allowed name (`org.example.App-3`) pass the same checks as the name itself, so an on-demand service or another app's name among them is never granted unchecked; and only the exact tray names SAM chose skip the check, not every name that starts like one. | 🆕 new |
| [`an_always_blocked_name_is_not_talked_to_and_others_stay_allowed`](../crates/sam-runner/src/dbus_proxy.rs#L555) | A name on the always-blocked list is not talked to although it is allowed, and blocking one name leaves the other allowed names alone (mutation run 2026-11-24: the comparison could be turned around unnoticed). | 🆕 new |
| [`host_tools_that_start_programs_outside_meet_their_stand_ins`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/sandbox.rs#L317) | Inside a real sandbox, the host tools that ask a service outside it to start a program (`systemd-run`, `flatpak-spawn`) are SAM's stand-ins: `flatpak-spawn` refuses, `systemd-run` runs the command right there, also where the tool is a link (contract host-escape-tools; audit D-M10: nothing ran `flatpak-spawn` inside a sandbox). | 🆕 new |

**Code:** [`sam-runner/src/dbus_proxy.rs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/dbus_proxy.rs), [`sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs), [`sam-core/src/permissions.rs`](../crates/sam-core/src/permissions.rs), [`sam-runner/src/kernel.rs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/kernel.rs), [`sam-core/src/host.rs`](../crates/sam-core/src/host.rs)

<a id="14"></a>
### 14. Display: Wayland with a security context; X11 only when allowed, with its cost explained.

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`wayland_security_context_socket_is_used`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4242) | With a Wayland security context, the app sees only that socket, not the desktop's own. | ✅ |
| [`no_compositor_or_no_answer_means_no_security_context`](../crates/sam-runner/src/wayland_ctx.rs#L220) | Security contexts count as supported only when a compositor says so: not where there is no compositor, nor where one hangs up without an answer (mutation run 2026-11-24: an answer of "always supported" went unnoticed; doctor and the contract check would then claim support wrongly). | 🆕 new |
| [`xauthority_gets_the_sandbox_display_number`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4212) | The X11 login cookie is rewritten for the display number inside the sandbox. | ✅ |
| [`the_x11_display_number_is_read_from_every_display_form`](../crates/sam-core/src/host.rs#L1081) | The X11 display number is read from every common DISPLAY form, and remote displays are refused. | ✅ |
| [`the_x11_key_copy_is_private_and_follows_no_link`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4346) | The app runs with the user's IDs, and host tools that could leave the sandbox (systemd-run) are replaced by harmless stand-ins. | ✅ |
| [`the_x11_cookie_is_passed_on_only_when_both_are_there`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9215) | The X11 cookie is passed on only when the host has one and SAM wrote the sandbox's copy: a cookie bind of a missing file stops the start, and a copy left from before is not the host's. | 🆕 new |
| [`display_variables_name_only_displays_the_sandbox_shows`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9249) | The display kind and the toolkits' backends follow what the app may use and what the host has: never a display the sandbox does not show, and none at all without a display. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_sandbox_display_number_is_never_the_hosts`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9350) | The sandbox's X11 display number is never the host's: 99, or 100 when the host's display is :99 itself. | 🆕 new |
| [`every_cookie_for_the_display_is_copied`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9364) | The cookie copy keeps every record for the host's display, wherever it stands in the file, and an empty file gives an empty copy. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_compositor_that_never_answers_does_not_hold_up_the_start`](../crates/sam-runner/src/wayland_ctx.rs#L242) | A compositor that takes the connection and never answers ends the attempt with an error once the time is up, instead of holding up the app's start for ever (audit: no time limit). | 🆕 new |
| [`each_permission_grants_exactly_its_access_in_a_real_sandbox`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/matrix.rs#L169) | Inside a real sandbox, each permission grants exactly its access and nothing more. | ✅ |

**Code:** [`sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs), [`sam-runner/src/wayland_ctx.rs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/wayland_ctx.rs), [`sam-core/src/host.rs`](../crates/sam-core/src/host.rs)

<a id="15"></a>
### 15. Sound, GPU, camera and game controllers only when allowed.

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`a_helper_sees_the_apps_system_and_nothing_else`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L3926) | A helper's sandbox (the sound relay) shows the same system as an app's (with the whole /usr), and nothing outside the system's own folders: no home, no runtime folder, no devices. | ✅ |
| [`a_hanging_relay_gives_up_and_a_finished_start_ends_its_relay`](../crates/sam-runner/src/sound_relay.rs#L339) | A relay that never opens its socket stops the start's sound within moments instead of holding the start; one that does is stopped, and its socket removed, when the start ends. | ✅ |
| [`nothing_in_the_sound_relay_can_make_a_user_namespace`](../crates/sam-runner/src/sound_relay.rs#L382) | Nothing in the relay's sandbox can make a user namespace: the relay reads what the app sends and, on PipeWire before 1.2, loads the modules the app asks for, and its sandbox let it make one (found while checking review K-2). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_relay_outside_a_sandbox_is_not_held`](../crates/sam-runner/src/sound_relay.rs#L250) | The lock check fails a relay that runs outside a sandbox: it sees the person's folder (mutation run P14: an answer of always "held" went unnoticed, so `sam-jail doctor` would not see such a relay). | ✅ |
| [`a_too_long_socket_path_is_named_before_the_relay_starts`](../crates/sam-runner/src/sound_relay.rs#L292) | A socket path too long for Linux is refused with a clear reason before PipeWire starts (it ended with an unclear crash: the 240-app run of 2026-11-20). | ✅ |
| [`a_launchs_sockets_fit_even_for_the_longest_app_id`](../crates/sam-runner/src/launch.rs#L3111) | Every socket SAM makes for a launch fits a Unix socket path (107 characters), even for the longest app id (one that grew twice for names that were taken), the highest process number Linux hands out and the longest user number (the 240-app run of 2026-11-20: apps with long ids lost their sound, bus and Wayland security context). | ✅ |
| [`the_sound_relay_check_needs_pipewire`](../crates/sam-runner/src/contracts.rs#L749) | The relay's quick check says "not here" on a computer without PipeWire, instead of reporting the contract broken. | ✅ |
| [`sound_comes_from_the_relay_or_only_when_allowed_from_the_server`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L3993) | Sound comes from SAM's relay, never from the sound server's own socket; that one only when the person allowed sound without the relay (audit S-F1). | ✅ |
| [`sound_never_loads_code_into_the_sound_server`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/sound_relay.rs#L98) | Sound never loads code into the sound server. | ✅ |
| [`the_relay_cannot_load_modules_and_listens_only_on_its_socket`](../crates/sam-runner/src/sound_relay.rs#L235) | The relay's settings switch module loading off, listen only on SAM's socket, take no D-Bus name and load no modules at start; a path cannot break out of its quotes. | ✅ |
| [`sound_reaches_the_app_through_the_relay_or_only_when_allowed`](../crates/sam-runner/src/sound_relay.rs#L412) | Sound reaches the app through the relay where PipeWire runs; without PipeWire only when the person allowed sound without the relay. | ✅ |
| [`sdl_gets_its_silent_driver_without_sound_access`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L3975) | Games built with SDL get a silent sound driver when they have no sound, so they start instead of failing: sound off, or on but with SAM's relay not running (audit S-F1); not when sound reaches them. | ✅ |
| [`gamepads_only_exposes_joysticks`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4793) | "Game controllers" exposes only joysticks, never keyboards or mice: also not a device that reports itself as a joystick and a keyboard or mouse at once, nor a node udev has no record for (review R56). | ✅ |
| [`every_controller_shown_is_watched`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4863) | Every game controller shown by name is watched while the app runs, with what it was when it was chosen; one already gone is neither shown nor watched (review R56). | 🆕 new |
| [`an_unplugged_controller_leaves_the_running_sandbox`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/device_watch.rs#L318) | An unplugged controller is taken out of the running sandbox; the app keeps running (review R56). | 🆕 new |
| [`another_device_at_a_controllers_name_is_never_shown`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/device_watch.rs#L332) | Another device at an unplugged controller's name (a keyboard that got its number) is never reachable: the node is taken out (review R56). | 🆕 new |
| [`an_unplugged_controller_leaves_a_sandbox_inside_pasta`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/device_watch.rs#L346) | With "This computer only" (pasta, which gives the sandbox its own process numbers) an unplugged controller is taken out too (review R56). | 🆕 new |
| [`the_launcher_takes_an_unplugged_controller_out_of_the_running_app`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/device_watch_launch.rs#L36) | Through SAM's launcher: an app with "Game controllers" gets the controller, and once it is unplugged the node is gone from the running app, which runs on to its end (review R56). | 🆕 new |
| [`an_unplugged_controller_that_cannot_be_taken_out_ends_the_app`](../crates/sam-runner/src/device_watch.rs#L599) | When SAM cannot reach the sandbox to take an unplugged controller out, it ends the app: Bubblewrap gave no status line, or one without the sandbox's mount namespace, or one no process has (review R56). | 🆕 new |
| [`no_controller_no_watch`](../crates/sam-runner/src/device_watch.rs#L687) | Nothing to watch: no watch at all. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_first_status_line_is_handed_on_at_once`](../crates/sam-runner/src/launch.rs#L2509) | The first status line reaches the controllers' watch as soon as it is complete, while the whole channel is still read to its end; without a line the watch hears that none comes (review R56). | 🆕 new |
| [`ending_the_sandbox_ends_it_at_once`](../crates/sam-runner/src/device_watch.rs#L655) | When SAM ends a sandbox it cannot keep its promise in, the program it started ends at once, whatever it does; once that program has ended, its number is never signalled again (review R56). | 🆕 new |
| [`sams_own_namespace_is_never_taken_for_a_sandbox`](../crates/sam-runner/src/device_watch.rs#L675) | SAM's own namespace (that of the person's whole session) is never taken for a sandbox, whatever the status line says: ending "the sandbox" there would end everything (incident 2026-11-25). | 🆕 new |
| [`without_landlock_an_unplugged_controller_ends_the_app`](../crates/sam-runner/src/device_watch.rs#L622) | Where taking the node out is not enough (no Landlock, or the app may make namespaces of its own), an unplugged controller ends the app (review R56). | 🆕 new |
| [`where_taking_out_is_not_enough_the_whole_sandbox_ends`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/device_watch.rs#L368) | Where taking an unplugged controller out is not enough (no Landlock, or the app may make namespaces of its own), SAM ends the whole sandbox at once (review R56). | 🆕 new |
| [`where_taking_out_is_not_enough_a_sandbox_inside_pasta_ends`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/device_watch.rs#L380) | … also inside pasta, which gives the sandbox its own process numbers (review R56). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_room_never_gets_a_watched_game_controller`](../crates/sam-runner/src/room_rules.rs#L2788) | A room never gets a game controller SAM watches: SAM could not take it out of the room when it is unplugged (review R56). | 🆕 new |
| [`with_nested_namespaces_an_unplugged_controller_ends_the_app`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/device_watch_userns.rs#L34) | With "Nested namespaces" on, SAM ends the app when its controller is unplugged, and says why; the app does not run on to its end. | 🆕 new |
| [`nvidia_modules_are_exposed_with_the_gpu`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4892) | With the GPU, the NVIDIA driver modules are visible; other kernel modules are not. | ✅ |
| [`each_permission_grants_exactly_its_access_in_a_real_sandbox`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/matrix.rs#L169) | Inside a real sandbox, each permission grants exactly its access and nothing more. | ✅ |
| [`sound_and_bus_data_come_with_their_permissions`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9154) | Without the whole system (`host_runtime` Minimal), the shared data of sound and of D-Bus is shown with their permissions, and only then; the sound settings in /etc in any case. | 🆕 new |
| [`device_information_comes_with_each_device_permission`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9181) | The device trees in /sys and udev's device data are shown with any one device permission that needs them, and never without one. | 🆕 new |
| [`device_numbers_split_into_major_and_minor`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9397) | Device numbers are split as the kernel numbers them: udev's data for a game controller is found under `c<major>:<minor>`. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_gpu_brings_exactly_the_render_and_nvidia_nodes`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4522) | With the GPU allowed the app gets the render nodes (`/dev/dri` as a whole) and NVIDIA's own device nodes; no other device the host lists is bound on its own. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_graphics_devices_are_found_in_the_device_folder`](../crates/sam-core/src/host.rs#L1239) | The graphics devices are everything in dri/ and the nvidia* entries, sorted, and nothing else of the device folder. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_graphics_devices_on_this_computer_are_found`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/host_probe.rs#L223) | The graphics devices SAM can give an app are this computer's: every entry of /dev/dri and every /dev/nvidia* entry, and nothing else. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_input_switch_names_every_device_it_shows`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L4765) | "All input devices" names every device it shows the app, and says what the virtual one allows: typing and moving the mouse as the person (audit: /dev/uinput was shown without being named). | 🆕 new |

**Code:** [`sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs), [`sam-core/src/host.rs`](../crates/sam-core/src/host.rs), [`sam-runner/src/device_watch.rs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/device_watch.rs)

<a id="16"></a>
### 16. The app can't make itself start later outside the sandbox (autostart, shell startup files, menu entries).

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`a_second_launch_never_takes_a_link_planted_meanwhile_for_the_users_own`](../crates/sam-runner/src/home_shared.rs#L300) | Review 2026-11-23 F1: app M plants `~/.bash_profile` (a link to its own script) while it runs; a second launch started meanwhile must not take that link for the user's own and put it back each time it is moved aside. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_running_launch_is_never_taken_for_a_killed_one`](../crates/sam-runner/src/home_shared.rs#L358) | A launch that still runs keeps its record, and what it watches is left alone: only a record whose launcher is gone gets the last look (mutation run 2026-11-24: taking every record for a killed launcher's went unnoticed). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_watch_read_back_from_disk_never_reaches_outside_the_home`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5770) | The watch kept on disk while an app runs comes back as it was, odd names included; a damaged record never names a place outside the home or the home itself, which the last look would move aside. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_start_file_left_by_a_killed_launcher_is_still_moved_aside`](../crates/sam-runner/src/home_shared.rs#L380) | Review 2026-11-23 F2: when SAM's launcher is killed (SIGKILL, a crash), its last look still happens: the next look at the running launches (`Sam::running`, which the window, `stop` and every start use) moves aside what the app left, and says so in its log. | 🆕 new |
| [`closing_an_app_that_ignores_it_still_finishes_its_launch`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/teardown.rs#L487) | Review 2026-11-23 F2: closing an app that ignores the request to quit still finishes its launch. | 🆕 new |
| [`grants_of_parts_of_the_home_never_reach_start_places_or_secrets`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/home_grants.rs#L69) | Grants of parts of the home never reach start places or secrets. | ✅ |
| [`a_writable_part_of_the_home_holding_start_places_is_watched`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L6927) | Only a start that shows a start place writable is watched and prepared: a writable `~/.config` is (audit D-M3), a writable `~/.config/myapp` or Documents folder is not. | ✅ |
| [`a_writable_grant_inside_a_start_place_is_bound_read_only`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L6327) | An extra writable folder that is a start place, lies inside one or is where one really is, is bound read-only, without, with a read-only and with a writable whole home, as named, through a link to the place and with `..`; the views count it read-only and the start says so. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_write_grant_in_a_start_place_is_found_as_named_and_as_reached`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L1737) | A writable folder counts as in a start place when it is one, lies inside one or is where one really is: as named, with `..`, through a link to the place, below a place kept in a dotfiles folder and below the places `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` names. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_write_grant_inside_a_start_place_is_said_to_be_read_only`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L1069) | An extra writable folder inside a start place gets a warning that SAM shows it read-only, named in the window and in `info` before the start; an ordinary extra folder does not (owner decision 9 of 2026-11-23). | 🆕 new |
| [`the_app_page_says_what_the_sandbox_does_with_a_grant_in_sams_folders`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L1123) | The app page decides about an extra writable folder in a place named like SAM's folders as the sandbox does, with where this SAM keeps its own (independent review of decision 9): this SAM's own `~/.local/share/sam` kept as a link to `~/Sync/sam` is not shown, through the link or by its real place, where the page said "Shown read-only" while the sandbox left the folder out; a folder by that name that SAM only keeps read-only (`~/.config/sam` planted as a link to the home) is shown read-only, and so is the same link's folder for a SAM that keeps its own elsewhere and a start that shows only a folder inside it. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_write_grant_in_sams_folders_outside_start_places_is_said_not_shown`](../crates/sam-core/src/warnings.rs#L1205) | An extra writable folder inside SAM's own folders outside any start place (`~/Games/sam/saves`, where SAM keeps its data, also reached through a link, or SAM's folders outside the home) is said to be not shown, as the sandbox leaves it out; a folder next to SAM's gets no word. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_write_grant_below_a_folder_the_app_can_change_is_not_bound`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L6416) | An extra writable folder below a folder the app can already change (another writable extra folder, the writable home, a folder opened writable) is not bound at all: a running copy of the app could turn a part of its way into a link to a start place after SAM looked, and Bubblewrap would follow it (independent reviews of decision 9). | 🆕 new |
| [`the_views_and_the_mounts_take_one_answer_per_write_grant`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L6871) | The views and the mounts take one answer per extra writable folder ([`write_grants`]): each is in the views exactly as it is bound (writable, read-only, or not at all), and the views of a start are those of its decided grants (independent review of decision 9). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_write_grant_inside_sams_folders_is_not_shown`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L6547) | An extra writable folder inside SAM's own folders is not shown at all, and the start says so in words that fit (independent review of decision 9: it was said to be shown read-only). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_write_grant_in_sams_folders_is_found_wherever_they_are_kept`](../crates/sam-core/src/places.rs#L1834) | A writable grant counts as in SAM's own folders when it lies in one of them wherever it is kept (`~/Games/sam`, also reached through a link), or in a start place that is one of them; a folder that only holds SAM's folder, or whose name only begins like it, does not (independent review of decision 9). | 🆕 new |
| [`full_home_access_cannot_leave_anything_that_runs_later`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/full_home.rs#L54) | Full home access cannot leave anything that runs later. | ✅ |
| [`the_home_protections_hold_whatever_else_is_granted`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/home_guards.rs#L88) | The home protections hold whatever else is granted. | ✅ |
| [`a_start_file_linked_into_a_read_only_user_folder_stays_locked_with_a_writable_home`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/home_guards.rs#L341) | Finding R63, attacked from inside a real sandbox: with the whole home writable and Documents set to read-only, a start file kept as a link into Documents (`~/.bashrc` to `Documents/dotfiles/bashrc`, as stow makes it) cannot be changed, through the link or where it leads, and its folder cannot be renamed away. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_start_file_linked_into_a_read_only_user_folder_is_never_writable`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L7300) | A start file kept as a link into a read-only user folder (`~/.bashrc` to `~/Documents/dotfiles/bashrc`) with the whole home writable is read-only: the folder's own read-only mount comes after the home (finding R63). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_new_start_file_never_stays_silently`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5819) | A new start file is taken out of its place whatever happens, and never silently (audit L-F2): past 1000 earlier copies, into SAM's folder; when SAM's folder cannot take it, renamed in place to a name nothing reads; when nothing works, reported as still there. | ✅ |
| [`start_places_follow_other_config_and_data_folders`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5868) | Start folders under another config or data folder in the home (`XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `XDG_DATA_HOME`) are protected like the usual ones, and the new places are on the list (audit S-F6). | ✅ |
| [`start_places_follow_config_folders_by_where_they_lead`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5903) | Start places follow `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and `XDG_DATA_HOME` also when they name the home's real place or hold `..`; a name that is not text is found, and a record read back holds only plain names (review B2 R53, R54). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_linked_config_folder_stays_watched_under_both_names`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5977) | A config folder reached through a link in the home is protected under both names, and the link itself stays watched: the desktop follows the name, so an app that swapped the link for its own folder would otherwise go unnoticed (independent review of PR 134). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_config_folder_whose_name_is_not_text_stops_the_start`](../crates/sam-sandbox/tests/odd_config_folder.rs#L12) | A config folder whose name is not text stops the start. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_folder_that_cannot_be_watched_is_reported`](../crates/sam-runner/src/home_live.rs#L232) | A folder Linux will not watch is said, not skipped silently (audit L-F4); what is in it is still moved aside when the app ends. | ✅ |
| [`paths_are_resolved_through_links_also_where_they_do_not_exist_yet`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5993) | Paths are resolved through links also where they do not exist yet, the way Bubblewrap and the app see them (audit S-F3). | ✅ |
| [`a_path_through_a_link_into_a_grant_is_visible`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L6008) | A path that leads into a granted folder through a link counts as visible, and one outside every grant does not (audit S-F3). | ✅ |
| [`a_missing_home_folder_stops_the_start`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L6024) | Without a real home folder nothing starts: full home access would otherwise bind the whole computer (audit S-F12). | ✅ |
| [`only_what_would_run_later_is_moved_aside`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5307) | After a start with a writable home, only what could make the computer run something is moved aside: a new startup file, a replaced link to a protected folder, a key folder the app was not given. | ✅ |
| [`a_new_startup_file_is_moved_aside_while_the_app_runs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/home_live.rs#L184) | A startup file created while the app runs is moved aside at once, not only when the app ends; a removed link is put back at once. | ✅ |
| [`a_start_file_left_in_the_home_is_moved_aside_and_named`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/moved_aside_message.rs#L25) | A start file left in the home is moved aside and named. | ✅ |
| [`protected_parents_are_locked_and_linked_keys_hidden`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5359) | With a writable home, the folders above protected places are mount points of their own (so they cannot be renamed away), and a key folder that is a link is hidden where it points. | ✅ |
| [`the_folders_above_where_a_linked_protection_lands_are_pinned`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5418) | A start file or credential store kept through a link (dotfiles, `stow`) is guarded where the link leads: every writable folder above that place is a mount point of its own, mounted before the guard, so the app cannot rename it away and put a file of its own where the unchanged link leads (review B2 S3, R50). | 🆕 new |
| [`the_folders_above_a_shown_credential_store_are_pinned`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5498) | The folders above each credential store are pinned with the whole home writable also when hidden files are granted and the stores are shown (`~/.var` above `~/.var/app`, `~/.cargo` above `~/.cargo/credentials`): nothing else guards them then, so without the pin the app could rename them away. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_start_file_kept_through_a_link_cannot_be_swapped_by_renaming_its_folder`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/linked_start_places.rs#L50) | A start file kept through a link cannot be swapped by renaming its folder. | 🆕 new |
| [`landlock_allows_writing_exactly_in_granted_folders`](../crates/sam-runner/src/kernel.rs#L772) | The Landlock rules allow writing exactly in the folders the permissions grant, a user folder at the access the sandbox shows it at: Documents read and write set to the same place as Pictures read-only is not writable (the stricter setting wins). | ✅ |
| [`every_breakout_attempt_from_a_real_sandbox_fails`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/breakout.rs#L156) | Every security promise holds inside a real sandbox: each breakout attempt fails. | ✅ |
| [`every_hostile_appimage_fails_to_escape`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/evil.rs#L159) | Every hostile AppImage, added and started like any other, fails to get out of its sandbox: it cannot read keys, SAM's settings or another app's files; cannot write autostart entries, shell startup files or host temporary files; cannot type into the terminal, reach the network when it has none, see host processes or change its own app folder; cannot reach systemd, the Flatpak spawn portal, the keyring or a launcher service, nor take over a portal's name on the desktop bus; and a symlink or setuid bit in its image gains it nothing. | ✅ |
| [`every_known_syscall_has_been_reviewed`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/syscalls.rs#L67) | Every known syscall has been reviewed. | ✅ |
| [`the_review_list_matches_the_filter`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/syscalls.rs#L89) | The review list says what the filter does with each syscall; it must say exactly that, or the review describes a filter that does not exist. | ✅ |
| [`an_app_can_never_replace_the_programs_that_menu_entries_run`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/own_programs.rs#L53) | An app can never replace the programs that menu entries run. | 🆕 new |
| [`sams_own_programs_are_read_only_wherever_an_app_could_write_them`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5698) | SAM's own programs are bound read-only onto themselves where the app could write them, also where the app sees them under the home's own name, and the folders above them are pinned; where the app sees them read-only or not at all, nothing is added (security review 2026-11-23, SB-1). | 🆕 new |
| [`sams_own_programs_are_its_appimage_and_the_programs_beside_it`](../crates/sam-core/src/util.rs#L840) | SAM's own programs are its AppImage, the running program and SAM's programs next to it, each once; other files there are not (security review 2026-11-23, SB-1). | 🆕 new |
| [`places_behind_links_stay_protected_and_the_app_still_starts`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/linked_places.rs#L65) | Places behind links stay protected and the app still starts. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_way_to_a_place_is_followed_link_by_link_as_the_app_follows_it`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5526) | The app's way to a host path is followed link by link as Linux reads it inside the sandbox: a relative link or one with a whole path, also behind another link and with `..`, ends where the sandbox shows the place; a link at a grant's own place is a mount point inside and not followed; a link out of what is shown, or a loop, ends nowhere (security review 2026-11-23, SB-6). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_way_through_forty_links_is_followed_and_one_through_more_is_not`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5588) | Both walks follow links as Linux does: a way through 40 links ends where they lead, one through 41 counts as a loop (reached nowhere, and possibly made by the app), and the check of a listed socket ends in time on a loop (the walk to a place has its loop test above). | 🆕 new |
| [`places_named_by_the_homes_real_path_are_protected_where_the_app_sees_them`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5631) | With the home kept behind a link (`/home/u` a link to `/data/u`), a link in it that names the home's real path (`~/.config` to `/data/u/dots/config`, as tools that resolve the folder they run in write it) leads nowhere inside, where only `/home/u` is shown; yet the app reaches the place it points to under the home's name (`/home/u/dots/config`). | 🆕 new |
| [`what_cannot_be_moved_aside_is_deleted_whatever_it_is`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L8777) | The last way out of a start place, when SAM can neither move nor rename what an app put there, deletes it: a file, a folder with everything in it, and a link (never what it points to). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_new_folder_above_start_places_is_moved_aside_once_as_a_whole`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L8804) | A start place that appears with the folder above it (`~/.config` and `~/.config/autostart` both new) is moved aside once, inside the folder above, not once more on its own. | 🆕 new |
