<a id="20"></a>
### 20. SAM checks from inside the sandbox that every setting applies (the sandbox check).

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`a_check_reports_what_it_finds_not_what_it_hopes`](../crates/sam-runner/src/probe.rs#L602) | Each check reports what it finds, not what it hopes: run on facts that are the same on every computer, a check whose expectation is wrong fails, one whose expectation is right passes, and the report counts them (found by mutation testing: a check turning every failure into a pass went unnoticed). | ✅ |
| [`a_skipped_check_is_neither_passed_nor_failed`](../crates/sam-runner/src/probe.rs#L751) | A verdict is exactly pass, fail or "could not check": a skipped check counts neither as passed nor as failed. | ✅ |
| [`the_check_expects_each_folder_as_its_settings_show_it`](../crates/sam-runner/src/verify.rs#L1223) | What the check expects of each folder: a granted user folder at its own setting, one without a setting as the home shows it (a read-only home shows Documents read-only even when Documents has no setting; a writable folder in a read-only home stays writable); keys only with full home and their own switch; extra folders like the home inside it. | ✅ |
| [`the_check_expects_a_user_folder_at_its_own_access_with_the_whole_home`](../crates/sam-runner/src/verify.rs#L1328) | With the whole home granted, the check expects a granted user folder at its own access, as the sandbox mounts it, not at the higher of the home's and its own: read-only in a writable home (finding R63), and outside the home read-only or writable as set (`XDG_DOCUMENTS_DIR=/data/Documents`). | 🆕 new |
| [`the_check_expects_a_refused_user_folder_as_not_shown`](../crates/sam-runner/src/verify.rs#L1426) | The check expects a user folder the sandbox refuses as the sandbox shows it: one reached through a link the app could have put in the writable home (`~/Downloads` a link to `/data/elsewhere`), or one set to `/tmp`, where programs outside the sandbox listen, is expected not shown, not writable through the home. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_check_expects_user_folders_at_one_place_at_one_access`](../crates/sam-runner/src/verify.rs#L1376) | The check expects user folders set to one place at one access, the one the sandbox takes: the stricter one, whatever their order (Music, Pictures and Videos all `~/Media`; owner decision of 2026-11-27), with the whole home writable, read-only or not granted. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_check_expects_no_folder_inside_sams_folders_wherever_they_are_kept`](../crates/sam-runner/src/verify.rs#L1535) | An extra folder inside SAM's own folders kept in a place of the person's choosing (SAM's data in `~/Games/sam`) is expected hidden, read-only or writable, with or without the whole home: the sandbox does not show it, and the check asks SAM's folders as the sandbox does. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_check_never_takes_the_home_for_sams_folder_through_a_link`](../crates/sam-runner/src/verify.rs#L1641) | A folder named like SAM's that is a link (`~/.config/sam` planted as a link to the home while SAM keeps its own elsewhere) never makes the check count the home as SAM's folder: a read-only and a writable extra folder in the home are expected shown read-only, as below any start place reached through a link, which is what the sandbox does. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_check_follows_the_sandbox_for_folders_named_like_sams_behind_links`](../crates/sam-runner/src/verify.rs#L1706) | The check counts another SAM's folders kept through a link (`~/.local/share/sam` a link to `~/Sync/sam`) as SAM's, as the sandbox does: a folder in them is expected hidden, read-only or writable, with the whole home and with a grant of `~/Sync`. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_check_expects_an_extra_folder_behind_a_planted_link_as_not_shown`](../crates/sam-runner/src/verify.rs#L1756) | An extra folder that the sandbox leaves out because the app could have planted the link on its way (the whole home writable, the link leading out of it) is expected not to be shown, read-only or writable; so is one whose link leads to a socket folder in `/tmp`. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_check_expects_a_left_out_extra_folder_as_its_target_is_shown`](../crates/sam-runner/src/verify.rs#L1844) | An extra folder the sandbox leaves out because of a link the app could have planted is expected as the other grants show the place the link leads to: the link itself stays visible through the home or a user folder, and the probe follows it. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_check_expects_a_user_folder_behind_a_planted_link_as_its_target_is_shown`](../crates/sam-runner/src/verify.rs#L2023) | A user folder the sandbox leaves out because the way there leads through a link the app could have planted (Documents set to `~/Sync/Documents`, `~/Sync` writable) is expected as the rest shows the place it leads to, here not at all; reached through a link you made in the home, it is expected as its switch says. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_check_expects_a_user_folder_linked_to_sams_runtime_folder_as_not_shown`](../crates/sam-runner/src/verify.rs#L1480) | SAM's runtime folder holds the sockets of SAM's helpers for every running app (the D-Bus proxy, the sound relay), also where it lies outside the host's runtime folder (`XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` unset, or `SAM_RUNTIME_DIR`): a user folder that is a link there is left out by the sandbox, so the check expects it not shown, read-only or writable, in a private home where no link counts as planted. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_check_expects_a_left_out_extra_folder_inside_an_ungranted_user_folder_as_the_grant_around_it_shows_it`](../crates/sam-runner/src/verify.rs#L1895) | With the whole home and in a private home, a user folder without a setting gets no bind of its own, so the check counts nothing for it where the folder really is: an extra folder left out for a planted link that leads into such a user folder outside the home (Documents set to `/data/Docs`, or to `~/Docs`, a link there, not granted) is expected as the extra read-only folder `/data` around it shows it, read-only. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_check_expects_a_left_out_extra_folder_inside_a_refused_user_folder_as_the_rest_shows_it`](../crates/sam-runner/src/verify.rs#L1953) | A granted user folder the sandbox refuses gets no bind of its own, so the check counts nothing for it where it really is: an extra folder left out for a planted link that leads into such a place is expected as the rest shows that place. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_check_judges_a_writable_extra_folder_by_what_is_shown_writable`](../crates/sam-runner/src/verify.rs#L1801) | The check judges a writable extra folder reached through a link as the sandbox does, by what is shown writable, and a read-only one by what is shown at all. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_check_expects_each_desktop_service_as_the_filter_shows_it`](../crates/sam-runner/src/verify.rs#L2262) | What the check expects of each desktop service that exists on the computer: full access reaches all of them; the filter reaches the desktop portal and exactly the names its list allows (a whole name, or a family with `.*`); a denied name stays hidden even with full access; a service the computer does not have is not checked at all. | ✅ |
| [`the_check_expects_the_rest_where_setting_and_computer_allow_it`](../crates/sam-runner/src/verify.rs#L2049) | Network, display, sound, the session bus, the SSH agent, variables and the security layers: the check expects each only where both the setting and this computer provide it. | ✅ |
| [`folder_check_tells_real_from_private`](../crates/sam-runner/src/probe.rs#L783) | The sandbox check tells a real folder of the user from a private stand-in. | ✅ |
| [`the_sandbox_check_passes_and_catches_mismatches`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/verify.rs#L23) | The sandbox check passes when the settings apply, and catches a setting that does not apply. | ✅ |
| [`a_wanted_layer_that_was_not_applied_is_reported_missing`](../crates/sam-runner/src/launch.rs#L2558) | A wanted layer that was not applied is reported missing. | ✅ |
| [`a_start_without_the_helper_reports_its_missing_layers`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/layers.rs#L32) | A start without the helper reports its missing layers. | ✅ |

| [`sam_jail_run_refuses_a_sandbox_whose_settings_do_not_apply`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/start_check.rs#L60) | Sam jail run refuses a sandbox whose settings do not apply. | ✅ |
| [`the_wanted_layers_follow_the_apps_switches`](../crates/sam-runner/src/launch.rs#L2836) | Landlock is wanted only where the app's switch wants it and the kernel has it; the app's strict filter switch works by itself, without a global setting (audit D-M13). | ✅ |
| [`the_helper_reports_the_socket_fence_on_its_own`](../crates/sam-runner/src/kernel.rs#L1485) | Review K-1: the abstract-socket fence is its own item in the helper's report. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_missing_socket_fence_is_reported_when_the_start_relies_on_it`](../crates/sam-runner/src/launch.rs#L2539) | Review K-1: a start that relies on Landlock's abstract-socket fence (it shares the host's network, the kernel has the fence) counts it as missing unless the helper reported it in place, also when Landlock itself reads "fully enforced" (a helper too old to report it). | 🆕 new |
| [`an_abstract_socket_that_answers_is_found_reachable`](../crates/sam-runner/src/probe.rs#L816) | Review K-1: the check for an abstract socket of the host really connects. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_check_tries_the_hosts_abstract_sockets_the_app_must_not_reach`](../crates/sam-runner/src/verify.rs#L2574) | Review K-1: with X11 off, the check tries the X server's abstract socket, which needs no file; with a session bus on an abstract socket, it tries that too unless the app may use the whole bus. | 🆕 new |
| [`checks_that_do_not_pass_end_with_not_passed`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/exit_codes.rs#L269) | In a real sandbox: `run --json` passes on the app's exit code, a `selftest` with a failing app ends with 3, and `verify` with a failed check ends with 3, so `sam-jail verify APP && sam-jail run APP` gates. | ✅ |
| [`a_passed_check_is_repeated_when_the_computer_changes`](../crates/sam-runner/src/verify.rs#L2394) | A passed check is reused only on the same computer as it was then: other GPU devices, the D-Bus proxy installed or removed, other sockets or another Landlock each make the check run again. | 🆕 new |
| [`selftest_passes_starts_that_ended_well_or_kept_running`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/src/main.rs#L1769) | `selftest` passes a start that ended well or kept running until the time was up, and fails one that ended badly or was killed early, with a second's grace for a kill from outside SAM (mutation run: every comparison here could be turned around unnoticed). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_selftest_ends_a_lasting_app_in_time_and_keeps_its_output_out`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/starts.rs#L166) | A selftest ends an app that keeps running once the time is up and passes it (instead of waiting for it forever); what the app prints goes to its log, not into SAM's answer; on the terminal a passed app gets ✓ and a failed one ✗. | 🆕 new |
| [`verify_checks_an_appimage_without_a_sandbox`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/starts.rs#L237) ⟲ | `verify FILE` checks an AppImage that has no sandbox yet, with the settings SAM would give it or, as the window asks before adding, with the settings given; no sandbox is created. | 🆕 new |
| [`verify_with_given_settings_is_refused_for_a_sandboxed_app`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/commands.rs#L683) | `verify --permissions-stdin` is for AppImages without a sandbox: for a sandboxed app it is refused instead of checking other settings than the ones given. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_apps_variables_reach_it_only_after_the_helpers_layers`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/launch_hygiene.rs#L131) | Review SB-5: variables from the app's settings (`environment.set`, rated Medium, or from an imported preset) reach the app, but never the loader of SAM's helper, which starts before Landlock and the syscall filter. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_apps_variables_go_to_the_helper_never_to_its_start`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L8676) | Review SB-5: with SAM's helper, the app's own variables (set ones, its AppImage variables, `$OWD`) are not given to Bubblewrap, whose first program is the helper, but handed to the helper to set for the app alone; SAM's own variables stay with Bubblewrap. | 🆕 new |

| [`a_start_whose_wayland_security_context_fails_does_not_run_the_app`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/missing_protection_stops_the_start.rs#L18) | A start whose wayland security context fails does not run the app. | 🆕 new |
| [`start_anyway_counts_only_as_the_first_argument`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/src/bin/sam-jail-run.rs#L500) | B2 R75: "Start anyway" counts only as the first argument, as the notice starts it; anywhere else it is a file for the app. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_start_anyway_button_starts_the_same_files_once_more`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/src/bin/sam-jail-run.rs#L518) | B2 R75: the notice's button starts the same app with the same files once more, each exactly as given (names that look like options, `--`, spaces), with "Start anyway" first; a `notify-send` without buttons still shows the notice, and nothing is started then. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_missing_layer_is_told_once_when_sam_starts_the_app_again`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/missing_layers_told_once.rs#L41) | A start that lacks the Wayland security context and is started again by SAM for its bus name calls the start's hook once, not once per try. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_menu_start_is_told_when_a_protection_layer_is_missing`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/src/bin/sam-jail-run.rs#L688) | Review K-4: a start from the menu is told at once when it lacks a protection layer, and the notification names each missing layer. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_check_is_repeated_after_the_kernel_or_a_sandbox_program_changed`](../crates/sam-runner/src/verify.rs#L2422) | Audit: a passing check is repeated when the kernel changed or a program that builds the sandbox is another build (an updated Bubblewrap, a rebuilt SAM helper, a new pasta), not only when SAM's version number or the settings did. | 🆕 new |

| [`the_check_names_the_wayland_security_context`](../crates/sam-runner/src/verify.rs#L2562) | B2 R78: the check names the Wayland security context, which SAM sets up outside the sandbox: failed when the desktop refused it, passed when it is in place, not named when there is none to make. | 🆕 new |

<a id="21"></a>
### 21. A second start, closing, and stopping work cleanly and leave nothing behind.

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`stop_closes_running_apps`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/stop.rs#L71) | Closing an app ends all of its running starts. | ✅ |
| [`a_launch_started_while_closing_is_left_alone`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/stop.rs#L180) | Closing an app closes the launches running when it began and waits only for those: a launch started meanwhile is neither asked to quit nor waited for, so closing ends once the earlier launch has (review B2 S6, R85: closing waited for every launch of the app, so a quick start again kept it waiting until it failed after 28 seconds, and its signals went to the earlier runner's process number long after the check). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_runner_is_taken_only_while_it_holds_its_launchs_lock`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/mod.rs#L1260) | A process is taken as a launch's runner only while it holds that launch's lock open (review B2 S6, R85). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_closed_runner_counts_as_ended_before_it_is_collected`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/mod.rs#L1282) | A runner signalled through its pidfd counts as ended as soon as it has ended, also before its parent has collected it, as the window or the command line may be (review B2 S6, R85). | 🆕 new |
| [`without_a_pidfd_a_runner_is_signalled_only_while_it_holds_its_lock`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/mod.rs#L1310) | Without pidfds (Linux before 5.3) a runner is signalled only while it still holds its launch's lock, checked right before each signal, and counts as ended once it holds it no more (review B2 S6, R85). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_program_whose_launch_has_ended_is_neither_waited_for_nor_signalled`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/mod.rs#L1357) | A runner whose launch has ended (its lock is free) while its program lives on, as a launch run inside SAM's tests or `sam-jail bench`, counts as done and is never signalled: closing would otherwise wait for the program that asked and then end it (review B2 S6, R85). | 🆕 new |
| [`sams_own_program_and_its_parent_are_never_signalled`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/mod.rs#L1383) | A runner that is SAM's own program (a launch run inside it, as in SAM's tests) is never signalled: its launch is asked to end instead. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_lock_reached_through_a_link_is_never_taken`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/mod.rs#L1421) | A launch's lock reached through a link is never taken as held, nor as held open by a runner: a planted link must not make another process's file count as a running launch (review B2 S6, R85). | 🆕 new |
| [`removing_a_sandbox_while_its_app_runs_is_refused`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/settings.rs#L220) | A running app keeps its sandbox: removing it is refused until the app is closed (a launch holds a lock in the app's runtime folder). | ✅ |
| [`the_overlay_check_leaves_no_folder_behind`](../crates/sam-core/src/host.rs#L205) | The overlay check leaves nothing behind, even the work folder the overlay locks (a leftover folder per check piled up in SAM's data). | ✅ |
| [`leftovers_of_earlier_overlay_checks_are_removed`](../crates/sam-core/src/host.rs#L223) | Folders left by earlier checks whose process is gone are removed; one of a check still running stays. | ✅ |
| [`without_network_the_sandbox_dies_with_its_launcher`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/teardown.rs#L164) | Without network the sandbox dies with its launcher. | ✅ |
| [`with_this_computer_only_the_sandbox_dies_with_its_launcher`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/teardown.rs#L173) | With this computer only the sandbox dies with its launcher. | ✅ |
| [`logging_out_closes_a_menu_started_app_properly`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/teardown.rs#L197) | Logging out sends SIGTERM to the launcher of an app started from the menu (audit L-F9). | ✅ |
| [`an_app_that_ignores_logout_is_ended_after_the_grace_time`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/teardown.rs#L294) | An app that ignores the request to quit at logout does not keep the launcher waiting: after the grace time (10 s) its sandbox is ended, and the start is still recorded as closed. | ✅ |
| [`closing_a_this_computer_only_app_gives_it_time_to_save`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/teardown.rs#L428) | Review 2026-11-23 F3: closing an app whose network is "This computer only" gives it its time to save, like any other. | 🆕 new |
| [`only_the_apps_own_processes_are_asked_to_quit`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/mod.rs#L1163) | Review 2026-11-23 F3: asking an app to quit reaches the app's own processes, also one whose program is called `bwrap`, and nothing of SAM's: not the program the launcher started (here a stand-in for pasta that quits on SIGTERM, which would take the sandbox down at once), not a helper beside the sandbox (as the sound relay). | 🆕 new |
| [`only_the_processes_below_a_sandbox_are_its_own`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/mod.rs#L1123) | The processes below an app's sandbox are its children and their children, and nothing else: quitting asks only those (mutation run 2026-11-24: the walk could take every other process instead, so stopping one app would have asked other sandboxes to quit). | 🆕 new |
| [`closing_an_app_that_ignores_it_still_finishes_its_launch`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/teardown.rs#L487) | Review 2026-11-23 F2: closing an app that ignores the request to quit still finishes its launch. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_running_check_at_the_start_never_ends_the_launch`](../crates/sam-runner/src/launch.rs#L2710) | A running-check (`lock_held`, as the window or `sam-jail list` run it) that meets a start at that moment never ends the start: while two threads keep checking, every one of many starts gets its lock. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_lock_held_too_long_stops_the_launch_in_bounded_time`](../crates/sam-runner/src/launch.rs#L2776) | A lock that stays taken longer than the wait still stops the start, with a message that names the lock, and in bounded time. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_start_and_a_change_of_one_app_exclude_each_other`](../crates/sam-runner/src/app_lock.rs#L218) | A start and a change of the same app never hold it together, in either order; another app is not held. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_change_inside_a_change_of_the_same_app_goes_on`](../crates/sam-runner/src/app_lock.rs#L257) | A change that is a step of another change of the same app (an update unpacks again) goes on in the same thread; another thread still has to wait until the outer change ends. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_lock_is_not_inside_the_apps_runtime_folder`](../crates/sam-runner/src/app_lock.rs#L323) | The lock file lives beside the apps' runtime folders, so removing an app's runtime folder never removes it. | 🆕 new |
| [`programs_sam_starts_never_inherit_an_apps_lock`](../crates/sam-runner/src/app_lock.rs#L276) | No program SAM starts inherits an app's lock: the lock files close when a child runs its program (close-on-exec), so an app never holds its own start lock and blocks changes for as long as it lives, and a helper of a change holds it only until it runs. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_sandbox_is_not_changed_while_its_app_is_starting`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/settings.rs#L257) | While an app is starting (from its checks until it has ended), its sandbox is not changed: removing it or unpacking it again waits a moment, then is refused and says why, and the sandbox stays (review L2). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_start_is_refused_while_the_sandbox_is_being_changed`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/settings.rs#L283) | While an app's sandbox is being changed, a start is refused at once and says why; the change itself goes on, also a step of it (an update unpacks again), and the app starts once it is done (review L2). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_start_sees_what_a_change_saved_before_it`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/settings.rs#L336) | A start reads the app's record again once it holds the app: what a change saved after the caller read it (a new unpacked copy marked "look at it first", or the sandbox removed) is what the start checks and runs (review L2: the mark was skipped). | 🆕 new |
| [`no_start_while_the_sandbox_is_being_changed`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/start_check.rs#L211) | A start from the menu or the window (`sam-jail-run`) and from `sam-jail run` is refused at once, saying why, while the app's sandbox is being changed (review L2: a start that met "unpack again" ran the new content without its "look at it first" mark). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_change_works_on_the_record_as_it_is_under_the_lock`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/maintain.rs#L2306) | A change works on the app's record as it is once it holds the app, not on the copy read when the change was asked for: another change may have saved in between (review L2). | 🆕 new |
| [`unpacking_for_a_start_is_refused_at_once_while_the_sandbox_is_changed`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/settings.rs#L303) | A start whose unpacked copy is gone (after "Free space") unpacks it again first; while another step changes the sandbox, that is refused at once with the start's message, as the start itself is (review L2). | 🆕 new |

**Code:** [`sam-runner/src/launch.rs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/launch.rs), [`sam-runner/src/ops/mod.rs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/mod.rs), [`sam-core/src/host.rs`](../crates/sam-core/src/host.rs), [`sam-runner/src/app_lock.rs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/app_lock.rs), [`sam-runner/src/ops/maintain.rs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/ops/maintain.rs)

**Also tried by:** [`tests/real-apps/run.sh`](../tests/real-apps/run.sh)

<a id="22"></a>
### 22. Logs of every start are kept, with old ones removed automatically.

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`the_newest_log_survives_pruning_within_one_second`](../crates/sam-storage/src/lib.rs#L843) | With more launches in one second than logs are kept, the newest log survives the pruning after its launch (regression: on a fast machine a pruned name was reused, sorted as the oldest and deleted at once). | ✅ |
| [`the_newest_log_is_found_even_within_one_second`](../crates/sam-storage/src/lib.rs#L1200) | The newest log is found even when two logs start within the same second. | ✅ |
| [`a_launch_log_stops_growing_at_its_limit`](../crates/sam-runner/src/launch.rs#L2454) | An app that prints without end cannot fill the disk or SAM's memory: the launch log stops at its limit with a note, a line without an end is read in pieces, and the whole output is still read. | ✅ |
| [`logs_shows_the_log_asked_for_and_nothing_else`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/commands.rs#L657) | `logs APP --file F` shows exactly that log of the app, and refuses a file that is not one of its logs. | 🆕 new |
| [`private_folders_and_logs_are_for_the_user_only`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/launch_hygiene.rs#L348) | Review F4 and audit A2: an app's private home and its own layer of the app folder, and the start's log, are for the user only, also with the usual umask (022) and when an older SAM made their folders readable for everyone. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_full_log_never_loses_the_starts_record`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/launch_hygiene.rs#L409) | Audit L-F5: when the app's output fills the start's log to the limit the system allows (a full disk the app may cause on purpose), the start still ends normally and its record is saved, with its result. | 🆕 new |

## E. Fixing apps that don't work

<a id="23"></a>
### 23. After a failed start, SAM says what went wrong and offers a fix with one click.

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`a_step_from_the_apps_output_needs_yes`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/src/main.rs#L1824) | A step from the app's own output needs --yes; SAM's own steps do not (audit U-M2). | ✅ |
| [`the_apps_own_words_never_give_it_a_bus_name`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs#L1004) | What the app prints never gets it a bus name, whatever name it claims it needs (a terminal's name runs host commands; audit D-M6): names come only from the D-Bus filter's record (`own_names`). | ✅ |
| [`a_start_without_bubblewrap_stops_before_anything_runs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/launch.rs#L2895) | Without Bubblewrap, starting an app stops while it is being prepared, before any process is started, and the error says what to install. | ✅ |
| [`sandbox_setup_errors_are_told_apart_from_app_errors`](../crates/sam-runner/src/launch.rs#L3013) | Bubblewrap setup errors are told apart from errors of the app itself. | ✅ |
| [`a_start_is_judged_by_whether_the_app_started`](../crates/sam-runner/src/launch.rs#L3034) | A start is judged by whether Bubblewrap announced the app, not by what the app printed. | ✅ |
| [`analyzer_maps_errors_to_fixes`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs#L1059) | Analyzer maps errors to fixes. | ✅ |
| [`fake_bwrap_error_is_not_a_sandbox_failure`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/adversarial.rs#L21) | H1: an app printing a fake Bubblewrap error is not a sandbox failure. | ✅ |
| [`only_a_failed_start_leaves_a_last_problem`](../crates/sam-core/src/model.rs#L1095) | The last problem names a start that failed by itself: the first finding, or else the exit summary. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_hint_applies_only_what_was_asked_for`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/commands.rs#L215) | `hints APP --apply ID` applies exactly the hint asked for: "files" grants Downloads and leaves the network off, although the game is also offered the network; an id that is not offered changes nothing. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_risky_hint_is_applied_only_with_yes`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/commands.rs#L249) | `hints APP --apply ID` applies a hint rated Think twice or Dangerous only with --yes, as `set` does: without it the network hint is refused with `hint-needs-yes`, names the hint and changes nothing; with it the network is on. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_risky_hint_needs_yes_through_repair_too`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/commands.rs#L290) | A risky first-run hint needs --yes through `repair --apply` as well: `repair APP --apply network` found the hint and applied it unasked (review of owner decision 13 of 2026-11-23). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_risky_hint_needs_yes_and_says_what_it_allows`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs#L1607) | A first-run hint rated Think twice or Dangerous needs a yes, whichever command applies it, and the refusal names the hint, what it turns on and what it adds; a Medium hint and an ordinary repair step do not (owner decision 13 of 2026-11-23; review of the first build: `repair --apply network` applied the hint unasked). | 🆕 new |
| [`a_risky_repair_step_is_applied_only_with_yes`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/commands.rs#L383) | `repair APP --apply ID` applies a repair step rated Think twice or Dangerous only with --yes, as `set` and risky hints do: without it turning off the syscall filter is refused with `step-needs-yes`, names the step and changes nothing; with it the filter is off. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_risky_repair_step_needs_yes_and_says_what_it_changes`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs#L1682) | A repair step rated Think twice or Dangerous, or one that turns on compatibility mode, needs a yes like a risky hint, and the refusal names the step, what it changes and the two ways to say yes; a step rated Some access does not. | 🆕 new |
| [`one_gate_judges_an_applied_step_in_one_order`](../crates/sam-runner/src/gate.rs#L363) | A hint or repair step applied to an app's record goes through the same gate, in a fixed order: what the app printed first (also under the name of a risky hint of SAM's own), then a first-run hint by its rating, then any other step by its rating in the ladder; a lower label handed in skips nothing, and a Some access step applies. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_hint_is_never_rated_below_what_it_grants`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs#L1435) | A first-run hint or repair step is never labelled lower than what applying it grants: for every hint and every step of the repair ladder SAM offers (apps built to trigger each one, alone and next to a home, a keyring or the network already allowed, with steps from what an app printed, on a computer that blocks Chromium's own sandbox and on one that does not), the rating is at least the highest level the change adds. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_repair_steps_label_is_never_below_what_applying_it_adds`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs#L1571) | Each of SAM's own repair steps, and the nested namespaces step its log analyzer finds, is labelled at least as the risk catalog rates what applying it adds, at every level: nested namespaces are Some access, not Harmless, and turning off the syscall filter is Think twice, not Some access (owner decision 17 of 2026-11-23: honest risk words; the labels were written by hand and contradicted the catalog). | 🆕 new |
| [`the_window_confirms_a_hint_exactly_when_it_asked`](../crates/sam-gtk/src/app_page/mod.rs#L961) | The window confirms a hint for the command line with --yes exactly when it has asked: a hint rated Think twice or Dangerous, or one that turns on compatibility mode, but not a Medium one (owner decision 13 of 2026-11-23: `hints --apply` refuses a risky hint without --yes). | 🆕 new |
| [`the_window_confirms_a_repair_step_exactly_when_it_asked`](../crates/sam-gtk/src/app_page/mod.rs#L999) | The window confirms a repair step for the command line with --yes exactly when it has asked, as it does for a hint: a step rated Think twice or Dangerous, one that turns on compatibility mode, or one from the app's own output. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_quick_failure_gets_a_notice_with_the_fix_button`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/menu_notice.rs#L193) | An app that fails right after it started gets a notice saying so, and its button opens SAM's window at that app's repair page. | 🆕 new |
| [`the_fix_button_opens_sam_only_when_sam_is_found`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/src/bin/sam-jail-run.rs#L655) | "Show the fix" opens SAM's own AppImage when SAM runs from one; with no SAM window anywhere there is no button (a plain notice), never a button that runs nothing. | 🆕 new |
| [`only_a_quick_failure_counts_as_stopped_right_after_start`](../crates/sam-core/src/model.rs#L935) | Only a failure within the first ten seconds counts as "stopped right after starting" (the menu start's notice with a likely fix): not a clean end, however quick, and not a failure after the app ran for a while. | 🆕 new |
| [`a_refused_menu_start_is_told_in_a_notice`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/tests/menu_notice.rs#L161) | A start SAM refuses before any sandbox is built (an app it does not know, a file name that is not text) is told in a notice, naming what went wrong; the start still ends with an error. | 🆕 new |

**Code:** [`sam-runner/src/repair.rs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs), [`sam-runner/src/gate.rs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/gate.rs), [`sam-runner/src/launch.rs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/launch.rs), [`sam-gtk/src/app_page/render.rs`](../crates/sam-gtk/src/app_page/render.rs)

**Also tried by:** [`tests/gui/app-page-atspi.sh`](../tests/gui/app-page-atspi.sh), [`tests/real-apps/run.sh`](../tests/real-apps/run.sh)

<a id="24"></a>
### 24. Repair steps go from mild to strong, and each one can be undone.

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`steps_that_add_are_undone_and_tried_steps_are_no_hints`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs#L1284) | Steps that add a list entry or an argument are undone by removing exactly that, and a tried step is not offered again as a first-run hint either. | ✅ |
| [`only_an_ordinary_start_judges_repair_steps`](../crates/sam-runner/src/launch.rs#L2683) | Only an ordinary start decides the repair steps on trial: not a traced or checking run, and not a start that failed for a bus name SAM just allowed. | ✅ |
| [`the_no_sandbox_hint_is_only_for_electron_where_it_is_blocked`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs#L1390) | The first-start hints offer starting without Chromium's own sandbox only to an Electron app that does not already start without it, and only where the computer blocks that sandbox (audit A1; mutation run P14: offering it to every app, or on every computer, went unnoticed). | ✅ |
| [`the_graphics_hint_is_only_for_apps_without_it`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs#L1784) | The graphics hint is only for a windowed app that has no graphics card yet and showed no sign of how it draws (mutation run of 2026-11-21: offering it to every windowed app went unnoticed). | ✅ |
| [`the_chromium_sandbox_check_matches_what_this_computer_allows`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs#L1818) | What SAM reads from the kernel's settings matches what this computer really allows: a program creating its own sandbox (a user namespace with a network namespace in it, as Chromium's sandbox does) works exactly where SAM says it is not blocked. | ✅ |
| [`a_repair_step_is_kept_when_it_helps_and_undone_when_it_does_not`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/repair_trial.rs#L30) | A repair step is kept when it helps and undone when it does not. | ✅ |
| [`a_repair_step_that_did_not_help_is_undone`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs#L1227) | A step on trial is kept when the next start works and undone when it fails: exactly what it changed is put back, it is remembered as tried and not offered again; a step from before (not on trial) is never touched (owner decision of 2026-11-18; night run N3). | ✅ |
| [`the_ladder_offers_only_what_can_help_and_says_what_it_costs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs#L1340) | Starting without Chromium's own sandbox is offered only where this computer blocks it (audit A1), compatibility mode is Dangerous and says it is a way out (D-H5), and "never offer compatibility mode" keeps it out (U-M9). | ✅ |
| [`compatibility_mode_needs_yes_and_says_why`](../crates/sam-jail-cli/src/main.rs#L1812) | Compatibility mode goes on only with --yes, and the refusal says why (audit D-H5); turning it off needs nothing. | ✅ |
| [`a_repair_step_says_what_it_changes_and_costs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs#L963) | A repair step says in a sentence what it turns on or off, with the switch's own explanation of what that allows. | ✅ |
| [`ladder_skips_applied_and_noop_steps`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs#L1193) | The repair ladder leaves out steps already applied and steps that would change nothing. | ✅ |
| [`a_writable_app_folder_is_offered_only_where_it_can_work`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs#L1842) | The repair steps offer a writable app folder only where an overlay can work: not where SAM's overlay folder cannot hold one (regression: the step was offered by Bubblewrap's version alone, and on such disks the app then started read-only anyway). | ✅ |
| [`undoing_repair_steps_restores_the_settings_from_before_the_first_step`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/settings.rs#L59) | Undoing repair steps restores the settings from before the first step. | ✅ |
| [`switching_off_one_repair_step_keeps_the_others`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/settings.rs#L432) | A repair step can be switched off on its own: exactly what that step changed goes back, and the other applied steps stay. | ✅ |
| [`only_plain_library_names_are_accepted`](../crates/sam-core/src/permissions.rs#L1819) | Only plain shared-library file names are accepted as system libraries: no folders, no `..`, nothing that is not a library. | ✅ |
| [`system_copies_replace_only_bundled_libraries_by_plain_name`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L5178) | A bundled library listed in advanced.system_libraries is replaced by the system's copy at its place in the app folder; names with folders or `..`, libraries the app does not bundle and libraries the system does not have are ignored. | ✅ |
| [`undoing_repair_steps_keeps_what_the_person_changed_since`](../crates/sam-runner/tests/settings.rs#L86) | Undoing repair steps takes back only what the steps changed: a setting the person changed afterwards stays as they set it, also the one a step had changed (audit G-F4: the whole profile from before the first step came back). | ✅ |
| [`only_a_system_file_replaces_a_bundled_library`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs#L9118) | A bundled library is replaced only by a plain file of the system's own (the copy found is checked by `system_copies_replace_only_bundled_libraries_by_plain_name`): never by a file elsewhere, nor by a folder. | 🆕 new |

**Code:** [`sam-runner/src/repair.rs`](../crates/sam-runner/src/repair.rs), [`sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs`](../crates/sam-sandbox/src/lib.rs)

<a id="25"></a>
### 25. The diagnose run finds missing files and suggests only what helps.

| Test | Promise | Result |
|---|---|---|
| [`failed_accesses_are_read_from_a_trace`](../crates/sam-runner/src/diagnose.rs#L563) | Failed file and socket accesses are read from a diagnostic trace. | ✅ |
| [`failed_accesses_map_to_the_permission_that_fixes_them`](../crates/sam-runner/src/diagnose.rs#L583) | Failed accesses are mapped to the permission that would fix them (X11 socket, GPU device), and unknown paths to none. | ✅ |
| [`a_failure_despite_the_permission_is_not_suggested_again`](../crates/sam-runner/src/diagnose.rs#L624) | A GPU device that fails to open while the GPU is already allowed is reported as "already granted", not as a suggestion to allow the GPU (regression: neovide on NVIDIA got "Enable GPU acceleration" twice). | ✅ |
| [`the_apps_own_folders_in_its_private_home_are_not_reported`](../crates/sam-runner/src/diagnose.rs#L646) | A missing cache, settings or data folder in the app's private home is the app's own and is not reported, while a missing document folder is (regression: ~/.cache/nvidia was suggested as a folder to grant). | ✅ |
| [`failed_changes_to_a_file_are_not_missing_access`](../crates/sam-runner/src/diagnose.rs#L663) | A failed attempt to change a file's mode is not missing access (regression: NVIDIA's driver chmods /dev/nvidia-modeset, which fails for every ordinary user, and diagnose reported it as a GPU problem). | ✅ |
| [`write_checks_on_system_folders_are_not_missing_access`](../crates/sam-runner/src/diagnose.rs#L675) | Checking whether a system file is writable is not missing access (regression: Zrythm and mpv ask access(W_OK) for files in /usr, which fails for every user, and diagnose suggested granting /usr/lib/lv2). | ✅ |
| [`files_in_etc_are_suggested_and_not_added_automatically`](../crates/sam-runner/src/diagnose.rs#L691) | A file in /etc is suggested, never added by itself, because it describes this computer (regression: /etc/fstab and /etc/pacman.conf were "low risk" and added automatically, although the apps ran without them). | ✅ |
| [`the_apps_own_settings_folder_is_not_reported_as_sensitive`](../crates/sam-runner/src/diagnose.rs#L723) | The apps own settings folder is not reported as sensitive. | ✅ |
| [`diagnostic_runs_obey_the_preference`](../crates/sam-runner/src/diagnose.rs#L706) | The app's own settings folder in its private home is not reported as a sensitive folder (regression: ~/.config was "dangerous"). | ✅ |
| [`the_trace_is_read_only_as_a_plain_file_of_limited_size`](../crates/sam-runner/src/diagnose.rs#L535) ⟲ | Regression (audit U-M3): SAM read the trace, which lies in a folder the app can write, with `read_to_string` through links. | 🆕 new |
